## 1. THE DEFECT, AND WHY THIS ROUND TAKES FIVE SITES AS ONE
`DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)` (the DC21285 core-logic device of `-E cats`, at physical `0x42000000`) still ends the
emulator process at SEVEN guest-reachable sites after #539.  FIVE are accesses in a direction the datasheet does not
define for the register: a WRITE to a read-only status register (IRQ_STATUS +0x180, IRQ_RAW_STATUS +0x184, FIQ_STATUS
+0x280, FIQ_RAW_STATUS +0x284) and a READ of a write-only register (IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR +0x18c).  The other two are READS
of SDRAM_BA_MASK/OFFSET (+0x100/+0x104), documented READ/WRITE registers whose write arms discard the value -- a
different class (a missing value, not an undefined direction) and a SEPARATE round (`fbsdrampair`).  The five are ONE
class under the reopening rule: same device, same semantic class (undefined direction -> the model refuses), same
detector (`regress/footbridge_sites_probe.py`), one fix design.

The datasheet, verbatim (`_scratchpad/dc21285.txt`, Intel 278115-001):

and the CSR access rule:
 1651|        The selected CSR (the CSR with offset equal to SA-110 address A[10:2]) read data is driven onto
 1652|        D[31:0]. If a nonexistent CSR is selected within the CSR address range, zeros are read (no error
 1653|        action is taken). The SA-110 normally stalls for three cycles.

## 2. PREMISES ALREADY SETTLED, RE-INLINED SO NOBODY SPENDS AN ANSWER RE-DERIVING THEM
Settled by #539 (n6): pass 1 `panel_20260919_005103`, pass 2 `panel_20260919_021100`, F2 `panel_20260919_035324`, the
fable5 rulings `_scratchpad/n6/fable5_p1_20260919.md` / `fable5_f2_20260919.md`, the codex M1/M2/K4 readings:
* **#539's kill bridge is TEMPORARY and THIS round retires it** -- fable5 D1: the three probes' "the device can still
  end the process" control is a READ of +0x18c (`ldr r7,[r0,#396]` = 0xE590718C, complaint text `ENABLE CLEAR`, K rows
  require the complaint AND exit 1); the round that declines +0x18c builds the durable harness legs IN THE SAME COMMIT.
* **The durable legs (codex M1's oracle table, adopted by fable5 as the spec):** K1 startup-rejection -- launch with an
  unused `-E` name through each probe's OWN launcher; no prompt; the unknown-emulation diagnostic names that input;
  `WIFEXITED && WEXITSTATUS == 1`.  K2 death-after-execution -- the prologue verified complete and live by a fresh
  `reg` (MMU bit clear, RAM 0x11223344, VENDOR 0x1011, the timer-mask value), then `quit`; the next ordinary interaction
  fails with `dead` BEFORE cleanup; `WEXITSTATUS == 0`.  Every surviving arm reaps exit 0.  There are THREE separately
  implemented launchers (sites' nested helpers; drain's and witness's `Session` classes): the legs go in each.
* **Device reachability is carried by value rows, not by a kill** -- A1 (VENDOR_ID 0x1011), A2 (TIMER_1_LOAD masked
  to 24 bits), and #539's E rows (E1/E2 `|=` accumulation at +0x188).  `quit` exits 0 through normal shutdown
  (`debugger_cmds.c` sets `emul_shutdown`/`exit_debugger`; `main()` returns 0).
* **The teardown as shipped by #539** (all three probes): `quit`, drain the pty to EOF (bounded 20 s; `rd()` returns
  True on a select timeout and False only on EOF/EIO), close, bounded WNOHANG reap (10 s), SIGKILL only if still
  there and not already reaped, `exit`/`signal` on every session-return path, harness kills labelled
  `HARNESS-KILL(9)` / `HARNESS-CLOSE(<sig>)`.
* **+0x18c's decline must ALSO stop returning `d->irq_enable`** (codex M2, K4 D3): today the read branch computes it
  before terminating; a decline that keeps that assignment leaks the enable mask through a write-only address.
* **The VALUE warrant for a declined READ is 0** -- "nonexistent CSR ... zeros are read (no error action is taken)"
  (`:1652`); the diagnostic is emulator POLICY.  For a declined WRITE the datasheet defines no effect; the model must
  leave `irq_status`/`fiq_status`/the enables and the interrupt output UNCHANGED.
* **The house announce idiom** (censused 2026-09-17): a per-instance latch field + `fatal()` whose text carries an
  explicit suppression marker, `(once)` being the majority spelling; `fatal()` cannot be silenced by `-q`, which is why
  a guest-drivable diagnostic needs a latch and not a verbosity flag.  K4 proposed ONE `uint32_t warned_access`
  bit-field (bits 0-4 for these five) rather than five ints -- a proposal, not settled.
* **The default arm** (`default:` in the same switch) prints `[ footbridge: read from 0x%x ]` on every read that
  reaches it and returns 0 -- unlatched, per access, no exit.  Not this round's site; named so a decline is not
  modelled on it by accident.
* **Sub-word accesses**: the handler receives `len` and the unaligned `relative_addr`; word-aligned cases only.  The
  E3/E6 rows measured that a byte/halfword read at an aligned offset returns the low lanes.
* **What `exit_census.py` will count**: dev_footbridge.c carries ten live `exit(1)` at HEAD 9c28ca5 (HEAD lines 565,
  574, 606, 615, 624 -- this round; 714, 739 -- the SDRAM pair; 843 and 879 in DEVINIT, construction-time).

## 4. THE PROPOSED ROUND
**Fix (option-set member 3 for all five, DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE; member 2 silent decline is the alternative
per site):** each of the five arms stops calling `exit(1)`; the WRITE arms leave every field untouched; the +0x18c
READ arm returns 0 and no longer assigns `odata = d->irq_enable`; each arm announces ONCE per device instance with a
`(once)` marker.  Storage: K4's `uint32_t warned_access` (one bit per site, cleared by DEVINIT's memset) -- or five
`int` latches in the file's existing style; the panel chooses.  Texts (K4, verbatim):
  `[ WARNING: footbridge write to irq status? (once) ]`, `[ footbridge write to irq_raw_status (once) ]`,
  `[ WARNING: footbridge read from ENABLE CLEAR? (once) ]`, `[ WARNING: footbridge write to fiq status? (once) ]`,
  `[ footbridge write to fiq_raw_status (once) ]`.  0/5 share an exact message, so one aggregate boolean would let the
  first site suppress the other four -- one bit each.
**Detector rows (K4's A1-A5 / D1-D5, on `footbridge_sites_probe.py`; the SDRAM rows A6/A7/D6/D7 are NOT this round):**
  A1 IRQ_STATUS read +0x180 -> 0 with enable 0x30 (accept side; catches masked-AND -> OR: 0x30)
  D1 two writes of 0x10 to +0x180: state unchanged, the announce ONCE (catches a store into irq_status: raw/status 0x10)
  A2 IRQ_RAW_STATUS read +0x184 -> 0 (catches `odata = irq_enable`: 0x30)
  D2 two writes of 0x10 to +0x184: unchanged, announce once
  A3 IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR write 0x10 with enable 0x30 -> enable 0x20; a zero write preserves 0x20 (catches a deleted clear)
  D3 two reads of +0x18c with enable 0x30 -> 0, 0; enables unchanged; announce once (catches the retained
     `odata = d->irq_enable`: 0x30 -- and a clear-on-read)
  A4/D4 FIQ_STATUS +0x280 with fiq enable 0xc0;  A5/D5 FIQ_RAW_STATUS +0x284 likewise
  Each D row's "once" is a COUNT of the announce text across two accesses (0 = silent decline, 2 = unlatched);
  every row also requires the arm to SURVIVE with exit 0 (a remaining terminating path shows as a missing continuation).
**The kill bridge retires and the harness legs land in the same commit:** arm K's +0x18c read (sites), the drain's and
the witness's K arms are REPLACED by the K1/K2 legs above (through each probe's own launcher), and `gate_arm.sh`'s
"the one-instruction kill arm still dies" check is replaced by two checks pinning the legs.  After this round NO
footbridge access reachable by these probes ends the process except the SDRAM pair.
**Witness (rung 3):** the new rows on HEAD 9c28ca5 -- D1..D5 and D3's read die at their first undefined-direction
access (exit 1 with the site's complaint) -- red pre-fix, green post-fix with the same script.
**Gate:** `gate_arm.sh`'s footbridge section gains count + value pins for the new rows (the same shape as the E pins).

## 5. THE THREE CONTROLS' CURRENT KILL ARMS (what this round removes), verbatim
  161| KILL_LOAD = (0x2c, 0xE590718C, "ldr r7,[r0,#396]", "arm K: read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR (0x18c)")
  347| K = run_arm("K  kill      (MMU off,+18c)", MMU_OFF + BODY + [KILL_LOAD] + TAIL)
  231|         p.append((0x5c, 0xE590718C, "ldr r7,[r0,#396]",
  200| KILL    = (0x48, 0xE590718C, "ldr r7,[r0,#396]",
and the sites probe's K row:
  402| k_ok = any("ENABLE CLEAR" in f for f in K["fatals"]) and K["exit"] == 1
  408|          "exit=%s" % K["exit"], "ok" if k_ok else "FAIL"))
  411| results.append(k_ok)

## 6. QUESTIONS
**Q1 -- premise audit, as questions.**
 (a) For each of the five sites: is the datasheet direction as stated (quote the section)?  Is member 3 right for it,
     or is there a site where member 2 (silent) or member 4/5 (the model already computes something; a fault) is
     the honest disposition?  Is FAULT even available on ARM for this machine (grep the fork: `dev_unreadable`,
     `memory_rw` exception dispatch for ARM)?
 (b) One `warned_access` bit-field vs five per-site `int` latches: which matches the file's existing idiom (grep its
     latch fields), and does the bit-field create a "who else writes this field" collision hazard?
 (c) The K1/K2 legs: do they establish what the old kill arm established (death detected; death detected AFTER guest
     steps) in each of the THREE launchers, and is "the next ordinary interaction fails with `dead` before cleanup"
     observable through each probe's `send()`/`wait()` as written?  Quote the helper you checked.
 (d) After this round, is the statement "no footbridge access these probes make ends the process" TRUE?  Enumerate
     every `exit(` in the function and say which guest access reaches it.
 (e) Does anything in the tree read or depend on the five complaint texts or on `ENABLE CLEAR` (a gate grep, a
     hygiene allowlist, `gate_hygiene.sh`'s boot-log rules)?
**Q2 -- candidate weakenings (which row reds first, or escapes).**
  (a) a decline that keeps `exit(1)` at ONE of the five sites   (b) the announce unlatched (fires every access)
  (c) one latch bit shared by two sites (the second never announces)   (d) +0x18c decline that keeps
  `odata = d->irq_enable`   (e) a WRITE decline that stores the written value into irq_status anyway
  (f) a decline that ALSO swallows the legal direction of the same register (IRQ_STATUS READ returns 0 always)
  (g) the announce text misnames the register   (h) K1's startup leg passing on ANY nonzero exit
  (i) K2's quit leg passing when the child died BEFORE the prologue completed   (j) the latch cleared by a read
  (k) the harness legs implemented in one launcher only
**Q3 -- option-set completeness.**  DELETE / DECLINE / DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE / ACCEPT / FAULT / BACKED ACCEPT /
  IMPLEMENT.  Member 3 is proposed for all five.  Is there a member this brief did not weigh for any site?  Grep the
  fork for the sibling handlers' dispositions (`dev_footbridge.c`'s other arms; `dev_unreadable.c`).
**Q4 -- the harness change's own risk.**  Replacing a kill arm with two legs in three launchers is the largest
  harness edit of the campaign.  What is the smallest edit to the LEGS that leaves them green while proving nothing?
  Name the row you expect to stay green.
If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE -- say so.
End with the seat record: model, what you read, what you ran (nothing).


## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

 succeeded in 260ms:
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """#432: the DC21285 "footbridge" driven by REAL GUEST INSTRUCTIONS, with no rig.
3: 
4: WHY THIS EXISTS ALONGSIDE regress/diff_footbridge.c.  The offline differential calls
5: footbridge_effective_cycles() and dev_footbridge_access() directly, so it proves BEHAVIOUR
6: but cannot prove the device is REACHABLE by a guest -- it would stay green under a change
7: that unmapped it (a memory_device_register length, a DM_ flag, or machine_cats.c's
8: `addr=0x42000000` line).  Until this probe, R6 was THE ONLY CODE ROUND IN THIS TREE WITH NO
9: REACHABILITY WITNESS OF ANY KIND: no rig boots a footbridge and there is no m8online analog.
10: This is that analog.  It goes through the real address decode and the real memory_rw
11: plumbing, on a COLD DEBUGGER -- no boot, no disk image, no guest OS.
12: 
13: *** THE TRAP THIS PROBE EXISTS TO SURVIVE, and it is the whole reason for arm T. ***
14: machine_cats.c:131 calls arm_setup_initial_translation_table(), which (cpu_arm.c:239-255)
15: writes a section descriptor for i in 0..255 at VA (j<<28)+(i<<20) -> PA i<<20 -- i.e. the
16: TOP NIBBLE OF THE VA IS IGNORED and only the low 256 MB of physical space is mapped
17: anywhere.  The footbridge sits at PA 0x42000000, outside that window, so under the boot-time
18: MMU it is UNREACHABLE: VA 0x42000000 aliases to PA 0x02000000 and a load returns 0.
19: 
20: That is not a hypothetical.  The first version of this probe returned 0x0 on every device
21: row WITH ITS RAM CONTROL STILL GREEN, and 0x0 is exactly what "the device is not there"
22: looks like -- so a survival-shaped or absence-shaped assertion would have CONFIRMED THE
23: OPPOSITE CONCLUSION.  A RAM liveness row cannot see it, because VA 0x9000 maps to PA 0x9000
24: identically with the MMU on or off.
25: 
26: So the guard is not a comment, it is arm T: THE SAME PROGRAM WITH THE THREE MMU-DISABLE
27: INSTRUCTIONS REPLACED BY NOPS MUST NOT PRODUCE THE DEVICE'S ANSWER.  If arm T ever reads
28: 0x1011, the discriminator is dead and every other row here is meaningless -- and it goes RED
29: rather than silently green.  Arm L additionally reads CP15 c1 BACK after the mcr and asserts
30: bit 0 is clear, so the precondition is MEASURED rather than inferred from the store having
31: been issued.  Three separate statements, deliberately: the guest turned the MMU off, the two
32: arms really differ in MMU state, and only the MMU-off arm can see the device.
33: 
34: VALUES, NOT SURVIVAL (rounds 79/80, and m8820x_sites_probe.py's header says the same).
35: A row that only asks "did the host stay up" cannot tell a reachable device from an absent
36: one -- an unmapped read survives too, it just returns 0.  So the two device rows assert
37: values that ONLY dev_footbridge.c can produce:
38:   * A1 reads VENDOR_ID and requires 0x1011 (dev_footbridge.c `case VENDOR_ID`, the
39:     DC21285_VENDOR_ID assignment).
40:     Nonzero and specific: the hand-assembled-encoding trap this project has been bitten by
41:     yields 0 from a wrong register field, and 0 is the one answer this row rejects.
42:   * A2 writes 0x12345678 to TIMER_1_LOAD and reads back 0x00345678.  THE VANISHING TOP BYTE
43:     IS THE HANDLER'S OWN SIGNATURE: `case TIMER_1_LOAD`'s write branch (one case serves all four
44:     timers through the fold) stores `idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL`
45:     (0x00FFFFFF, dc21285reg.h:371).  RAM, or any decode that merely absorbed the store,
46:     would return 0x12345678 unchanged.  This row therefore distinguishes "the device
47:     handled it" from "the write landed somewhere", which A1 alone cannot.
48: 
49: THE KILL PAIR, which is this probe's own failability control.  Arms L and K differ by
50: EXACTLY ONE INSTRUCTION.  Arm K adds a guest read of IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR (base+0x18c), which
51: dev_footbridge.c's `case IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR` read branch answers with fatal() then exit(1) --
52: an UNFIXED site, used here rather than repaired, because a probe that reports SURVIVED for
53: every row is vacuous unless something in it can still die.  Arm L must live; arm K must
54: exit 1 and say so.  If both survived, the guest instructions are not reaching the device at
55: all and every SURVIVED verdict above is worthless; if both died, the probe is killing the
56: host for its own reasons.  The one-instruction delta is the entire argument.
57:   #539 moved this arm from +0x188 to +0x18c.  +0x188 (IRQEnable) is a DOCUMENTED READ-ONLY
58:   register (DC21285 Table 7-10; 7.3.31: "can only be changed by writing to the EnableSet and
59:   EnableClear registers", reset 0) and now answers reads -- rows E0-E6 below.  +0x18c is
60:   write-only per 7.3.33 (IRQEnableClear), so a kill there is a decline-class site still dying,
61:   and the same deletion spilled onto the CLEAR read branch -- with or without its diagnostic --
62:   makes K survive, so K1 reds on the exit status it requires.
63:   The bridge is TEMPORARY: the round that declines +0x18c retires it and builds the harness
64:   legs (startup rejection + mid-session quit, status reaped) in the same commit.
65: 
66: ARM E -- #539's ACCEPT rows, in a FOURTH arm so that arms L/T/K are byte-identical to what
67: they were.  E carries its own C1/A1 (E0 = 0 is also what an ABSENT device returns, so the
68: E rows are meaningless unless this arm demonstrably reached the device) and then:
69:   E0 read IRQEnable before any write            -> 0            (reset value, 7.3.31)
70:   E1 write P=0x2002 to Set, read               -> P            (readable; catches a bit
71:                                                                   swap, a constant, a wrong
72:                                                                   field, a masked value)
73:   E2 write Q=0x60000000 to Set, read           -> P|Q          (pins |=; bit 30 set so a
74:                                                                   diagnostic kept for the top
75:                                                                   bits fires here)
76:   E3 ldrb  +0x188                              -> 0x02         (low byte: len 1 reaches the
77:   E6 ldrh  +0x188                              -> 0x2002        same case; the CSR drives
78:                                                                   D[31:0] and the SA-110 takes
79:                                                                   the lane, DC21285 3.2.x)
80:   E5 write P to Clear, read Set                -> Q            (Clear works; = the E2 value
81:                                                                   with P removed)
82:   E4 no BRACKETED diagnostic naming footbridge anywhere in arm E -- the `fatals` regex is
83:      `[ ... footbridge ... ]`, the house diagnostic shape; a kept fatal() with its exit gone
84:      is caught; a diagnostic rewritten WITHOUT brackets or without the device name is NOT
85:      (listed under NOT COVERED below)
86: Execution order is E0,E1,E2,E3,E6,E5: E6 wants 0x2002, which is true only while P is set.
87: P and Q are loaded from RAM (DATA+8, DATA+12), like 0x12345678: P is not a rotated ARM
88: immediate (bits 1 and 13); Q is (0x06 ROR 4) and is loaded the same way for uniformity.
89: Values land in r7,r1,r2,r9,fp,ip; r1/r2 are A2's registers in arm L and are free here
90: because arm E does not score A2.  TESTED PATTERNS, stated so the contract is checkable:
91: P = bits 1,13 and Q = bits 29,30 only; bit 31 and every other bit are untested by value.
92: 
93: CONTRACT (what this detector is designed to catch, and what it is not).  On the SET read
94: branch: the exit reinstated (E0 dies), reinstated only when irq_enable == 0 (E0), a wrong
95: field/constant/mask/bit-swap (E1, E2), a width guard (E3, E6), a kept diagnostic (E4), a
96: diagnostic kept for bit 30 (E4; E2 if it exits -- bit 31 is never set here, so a bit-31-only
97: guard is UNTESTED).  On the CLEAR arms: a broken `&= ~`
98: (E5), the exit removed from the CLEAR read too (K1).  NOT COVERED: (a) no row observes the
99: CPU IRQ line -- an edit to the write arm's assert condition (`irq_status | irq_enable`) is
100: invisible to every row here, because cpu_dyntrans.c suppresses interrupt delivery under
101: `step` and CPSR I/F stay set; it needs a free-running arm with a positive delivery control,
102: filed with IRQSoft; (b) +0x189..+0x18b: real hardware selects the same CSR by A[10:2] and
103: returns lanes 1-3, the model routes them to `default:` and returns 0 -- a device-wide decode
104: deviation, filed, not rowed; (c) irq_status and fiq_enable are 0 throughout, so a mutant
105: returning either at +0x188 is caught by E1/E2 only through the value, not distinguished
106: from a constant 0.  (d) D1 compares the disassembly of every STEPPED word; the landing pad is
107: planted and never stepped, and a step that ends the process is not compared.  (e) E4 sees only
108: a BRACKETED diagnostic that names footbridge (the `fatals` regex); a bare-text diagnostic on
109: the read path escapes it.
110: TEARDOWN CONVENTION this file's bounded drain rests on: rd() returns True on a select TIMEOUT
111: and False only on EOF/EIO, so the post-quit drain loop breaks on EOF or its deadline, never on
112: a quiet interval (the same convention as the two pending probes' Session.rd).
113: 
114: EVERY PLANTED WORD IS CHECKED AGAINST THE DISASSEMBLY THE EMULATOR PRINTS AS IT STEPS IT --
115: not against a separate `unassemble`, because the step line is the instruction that actually
116: executed.  This project has a recorded incident where a wrong register field made a gate row
117: measure the wrong thing for months.
118: 
119: COST, measured: see PROBE_WALL below -- 4 cold-debugger launches, no boot.  A booting-rig row
120: was deliberately NOT written: no rig in this tree boots a footbridge machine at all, which is
121: the gap this probe closes, and adding one would put a load-sensitive wall-clock oracle into a
122: battery already carrying ~37.
123: 
124: usage: footbridge_sites_probe.py <gxemul-binary> <four-byte-raw-stub>
125: The stub is the same scaffolding gate_arm.sh writes for testarm: `cats` needs a file
126: argument to construct, and every word of it is overwritten before anything executes.
127: """
128: import os, pty, re, select, signal, sys, time
129: 
130: BIN = sys.argv[1]
131: STUB = sys.argv[2]
132: CODE = 0x00008000
133: DATA = 0x00009000
134: NOP = 0xE1A00000
135: NOPTXT = "mov r0,r0"      #  the EMULATOR's spelling of 0xe1a00000; it does not print "nop"
136: 
137: #  (offset, word, expected disassembly text, comment)
138: #  Spellings are the EMULATOR's own, not a manual's: "mrc 15,0,r0,cr1,cr0,0" and decimal
139: #  ldr/str offsets ("[r0,#768]" for 0x300) are what this disassembler prints.
140: MMU_OFF = [
141:     (0x00, 0xEE110F10, "mrc 15,0,r0,cr1,cr0,0", "read CP15 c1"),
142:     (0x04, 0xE3C00001, "bic r0,r0,#1",          "clear the MMU enable bit"),
143:     (0x08, 0xEE010F10, "mcr 15,0,r0,cr1,cr0,0", "MMU OFF -> VA == PA"),
144: ]
145: MMU_NOP = [
146:     (0x00, NOP, NOPTXT, "arm T: MMU LEFT ON -- the negative control"),
147:     (0x04, NOP, NOPTXT, ""),
148:     (0x08, NOP, NOPTXT, ""),
149: ]
150: BODY = [
151:     (0x0c, 0xEE113F10, "mrc 15,0,r3,cr1,cr0,0", "C2: read CP15 c1 BACK -- measures the state"),
152:     (0x10, 0xE3A00442, "mov r0,#0x42000000",    "footbridge base (machine_cats.c:64)"),
153:     (0x14, 0xE3A04A09, "mov r4,#0x9000",        "scratch RAM"),
154:     (0x18, 0xE5945000, "ldr r5,[r4]",           "C1 liveness: plain RAM, 0x11223344"),
155:     (0x1c, 0xE5906000, "ldr r6,[r0]",           "A1: VENDOR_ID"),
156:     (0x20, 0xE5942004, "ldr r2,[r4,#4]",        "load 0x12345678 from RAM"),
157:     (0x24, 0xE5802300, "str r2,[r0,#768]",      "A2: TIMER_1_LOAD <- 0x12345678"),
158:     (0x28, 0xE5901300, "ldr r1,[r0,#768]",      "A2: read it back"),
159: ]
160: KILL_NOP  = (0x2c, NOP,        NOPTXT,             "arm L: no kill site")
161: KILL_LOAD = (0x2c, 0xE590718C, "ldr r7,[r0,#396]", "arm K: read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR (0x18c)")
162: TAIL = [(0x30, NOP, NOPTXT, "landing pad -- planted, never stepped")]
163: P, Q = 0x00002002, 0x60000000           # #539 arm E values; loaded from DATA+8 / DATA+12
164: ENABLE = [                              # #539: the ACCEPT rows, order E0,E1,E2,E3,E6,E5
165:     (0x2c, 0xE5907188, "ldr r7,[r0,#392]",   "E0: read IRQ_ENABLE_SET before any write"),
166:     (0x30, 0xE5948008, "ldr r8,[r4,#8]",     "r8 = P"),
167:     (0x34, 0xE5808188, "str r8,[r0,#392]",   "IRQEnableSet <- P"),
168:     (0x38, 0xE5901188, "ldr r1,[r0,#392]",   "E1: read back -> P"),
169:     (0x3c, 0xE594900C, "ldr r9,[r4,#12]",    "r9 = Q"),
170:     (0x40, 0xE5809188, "str r9,[r0,#392]",   "IRQEnableSet <- Q"),
171:     (0x44, 0xE5902188, "ldr r2,[r0,#392]",   "E2: read back -> P|Q (pins |=)"),
172:     (0x48, 0xE5D09188, "ldrb r9,[r0,#392]",  "E3: BYTE read -> 0x02"),
173:     (0x4c, 0xE280AD06, "add sl,r0,#0x180",   "sl = base+0x180 (ldrh offsets cap at 255)"),
174:     (0x50, 0xE1DAB0B8, "ldrh fp,[sl,#8]",    "E6: HALFWORD read -> 0x2002"),
175:     (0x54, 0xE580818C, "str r8,[r0,#396]",   "IRQEnableClear <- P"),
176:     (0x58, 0xE590C188, "ldr ip,[r0,#392]",   "E5: read back -> Q"),
177:     (0x5c, NOP, NOPTXT, "landing pad -- planted, never stepped"),
178: ]
179: REGNAME = {10: "sl", 11: "fp", 12: "ip"}   # the emulator's spellings (cpu_arm.h ARM_REG_NAMES)
180: 
181: 
182: def run_arm(label, prog):
183:     """Plant `prog`, step it one instruction at a time, report registers and liveness."""
184:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
185:     if pid == 0:
186:         os.execvp(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-T", "-E", "cats", "-M", "32",
187:                         "0x%x:%s" % (CODE, STUB)])
188:         os._exit(127)
189:     buf = ""
190:     dead = [False]
191: 
192:     def rd(t=0.4):
193:         nonlocal buf
194:         r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], t)
195:         if fd not in r:
196:             return True
197:         try:
198:             d = os.read(fd, 65536)
199:         except OSError:
200:             dead[0] = True
201:             return False
202:         if not d:
203:             dead[0] = True
204:             return False
205:         buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
206:         return True
207: 
208:     #  Signature order is not free choice: gate_hygiene.sh pins the CANONICAL #392 call form
209:     #  as a literal `return wait(mark=_mark, echo=<x> if <x> else None)`, tightened three
210:     #  times because every looser prefix was a substring of the broken form too.  Written any
211:     #  other way, a probe is simply not covered by that ratchet -- which is worse than failing
212:     #  it.  So timeout goes last, and send() ends on the pinned form.
213:     def wait(mark=0, echo=None, timeout=60):
214:         t = time.time()
215:         while time.time() - t < timeout:
216:             if not rd():
217:                 return False
218:             resp = buf[mark:]
219:             if echo is not None and echo not in resp:
220:                 continue
221:             if len(buf) > mark and resp.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
222:                 return True
223:         return False
224: 
225:     def send(x, timeout=60):
226:         b = (x + "\n").encode("latin1")
227:         _mark = len(buf)
228:         n = 0
229:         try:
230:             while n < len(b):
231:                 n += os.write(fd, b[n:])
232:         except OSError:
233:             dead[0] = True
234:             return False
235:         return wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None)
236: 
237:     status, regs, badis = "?", {}, []
238:     #  KEYWORD, and this is not style.  A positional wait(120) silently becomes mark=120 under
239:     #  the pinned parameter order -- a real behaviour change, and the exact mistake the
240:     #  m8820x probe's conversion had to fix in the same commit.
241:     if not wait(timeout=120):
242:         status = "NO-PROMPT"
243:     else:
244:         send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA, 0x11223344))
245:         send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + 4, 0x12345678))
246:         send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + 8, P))
247:         send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + 12, Q))
248:         for off, word, _t, _c in prog:
249:             send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + off, word))
250:         send("pc=0x%08x" % CODE)
251:         status = "SURVIVED"
252:         for off, word, text, _c in prog[:-1]:      # the landing pad is never stepped
253:             mark = len(buf)
254:             if not send("step 1", timeout=30) or dead[0]:
255:                 status = "HOST-DIED"
256:                 break
257:             seg = buf[mark:]
258:             #  Match the emulator's own step line for THIS address and compare the mnemonic
259:             #  it printed against what this table says was planted.
260:             m = re.search(r"%08x:\s+%08x\s+([^\r\n<]+)" % (CODE + off, word), seg)
261:             got = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", m.group(1)).strip() if m else "(no step line)"
262:             if got != text:
263:                 badis.append("0x%02x planted 0x%08x -> %r, expected %r"
264:                              % (off, word, got, text))
265:         if status == "SURVIVED":
266:             mark = len(buf)
267:             if send("reg", timeout=30) and not dead[0]:
268:                 for n in (0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12):
269:                     mm = re.search(r"\b%s\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)"
270:                                    % REGNAME.get(n, "r%d" % n), buf[mark:])
271:                     if mm:
272:                         regs[n] = int(mm.group(1), 16)
273:             else:
274:                 status = "HOST-DIED" if dead[0] else "NO-REG"
275:     #  #539 (pass 2): the SAME teardown as the two pending probes -- quit, DRAIN to EOF (bounded), close
276:     #  the master, then a BOUNDED reap (poll 10 s, kill, wait).  The old sleep-0.3-then-close could SIGHUP a
277:     #  child still shutting down and then block forever in waitpid; and fatals are collected AFTER the
278:     #  drain so a diagnostic printed during shutdown is not missed.
279:     try:
280:         os.write(fd, b"quit\n")
281:     except OSError:
282:         pass
283:     t1 = time.time()
284:     eof = False
285:     while time.time() - t1 < 20.0:
286:         if not rd(0.1):
287:             eof = True
288:             break
289:     fatals = re.findall(r"(\[[^\r\n\]]*footbridge[^\r\n\]]*\])", buf)
290:     try:
291:         os.close(fd)
292:     except OSError:
293:         pass
294:     rpid, st, gone = 0, None, False
295:     t1 = time.time()
296:     while rpid == 0 and time.time() - t1 < 10.0:
297:         try:
298:             rpid, st = os.waitpid(pid, os.WNOHANG)
299:         except OSError:
300:             gone = True      # ECHILD: already reaped elsewhere -- its pid may be recycled; never kill it
301:             break
302:         if rpid == 0:
303:             time.sleep(0.05)
304:     harness_killed = False
305:     if rpid == 0 and not gone:
306:         #  F2 (#539): a kill WE fire is labelled (see the pending probes' close()): 20 s drain + 10 s reap.
307:         harness_killed = True
308:         try:
309:             os.kill(pid, signal.SIGKILL)
310:         except OSError:
311:             pass
312:         try:
313:             _, st = os.waitpid(pid, 0)
314:         except OSError:
315:             st = None
316:     code = os.WEXITSTATUS(st) if st is not None and os.WIFEXITED(st) else None
317:     sig = os.WTERMSIG(st) if st is not None and os.WIFSIGNALED(st) else None
318:     if harness_killed and sig == signal.SIGKILL:
319:         sig = "HARNESS-KILL(%s)" % sig      # our kill, not a product death (a kill that lands on
320:                                              # a zombie reaps the product's own status: unlabelled)
321:     elif not eof and sig is not None:
322:         sig = "HARNESS-CLOSE(%s)" % sig   # the drain deadline expired; our close HUP'd a live child
323:     print("  arm %-36s %-9s exit=%-5s signal=%s" % (label, status, code, sig))
324:     for b in badis:
325:         print("      DISASSEMBLY MISMATCH: %s" % b)
326:     return {"status": status, "regs": regs, "exit": code, "signal": sig,
327:             "fatals": fatals, "badis": badis}
328: 
329: 
330: def hexs(v):
331:     return "-" if v is None else "0x%08x" % v
332: 
333: 
334: def row(tag, note, got, want):
335:     ok = got == want
336:     print("%-4s %-40s got=%-12s want=%-12s %s"
337:           % (tag, note, hexs(got), hexs(want), "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
338:     return ok

1630:        SA-110 Originated Transactions
1631: 
1632:        The following sections describe SA-110 originated bus transactions. These transactions are
1633:        classified by their address spaces (refer to Table 5-1).
1634: 
1635:        The 21285 controls the SA-110 clock, MCLK, by stretching the high time to stall the SA-110
1636:        during bus cycles (indicated by assertion of nMREQ by SA-110). The number of cycles that
1637:        MCLK stays high may be different for each address space and depends upon other 21285 activity
1638:        at the time nMREQ asserts.
1639: 
1640:        CSR Write
1641: 
1642:        The SA-110 write data is written to the selected CSR (the CSR with offset equal to SA-110 address
1643:        A[10:2]). The SA-110 is normally stalled for three cycles, but can be stalled longer. If a
1644:        nonexistent CSR is selected within the CSR address range, the write data is discarded (no error
1645:        action is taken).
1646: 
1647:        The CSR address space is listed in Tables 7-2, 7-3, and 7-4.
1648: 
1649:        CSR Read
1650: 
1651:        The selected CSR (the CSR with offset equal to SA-110 address A[10:2]) read data is driven onto
1652:        D[31:0]. If a nonexistent CSR is selected within the CSR address range, zeros are read (no error
1653:        action is taken). The SA-110 normally stalls for three cycles.
1654: 
1655: 21285 Core Logic for SA-110 Datasheet  3-1
1656: Transactions
1657: 
1658:                    The CSR address space is listed in Tables 7-2, 7-3, and 7-4.

182: def run_arm(label, prog):
183:     """Plant `prog`, step it one instruction at a time, report registers and liveness."""
184:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
185:     if pid == 0:
186:         os.execvp(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-T", "-E", "cats", "-M", "32",
187:                         "0x%x:%s" % (CODE, STUB)])
188:         os._exit(127)
189:     buf = ""
190:     dead = [False]
191: 
192:     def rd(t=0.4):
193:         nonlocal buf
194:         r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], t)
195:         if fd not in r:
196:             return True
197:         try:
198:             d = os.read(fd, 65536)
199:         except OSError:
200:             dead[0] = True
201:             return False
202:         if not d:
203:             dead[0] = True
204:             return False
205:         buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
206:         return True
207: 
208:     #  Signature order is not free choice: gate_hygiene.sh pins the CANONICAL #392 call form
209:     #  as a literal `return wait(mark=_mark, echo=<x> if <x> else None)`, tightened three
210:     #  times because every looser prefix was a substring of the broken form too.  Written any
211:     #  other way, a probe is simply not covered by that ratchet -- which is worse than failing
212:     #  it.  So timeout goes last, and send() ends on the pinned form.
213:     def wait(mark=0, echo=None, timeout=60):
214:         t = time.time()
215:         while time.time() - t < timeout:
216:             if not rd():
217:                 return False
218:             resp = buf[mark:]
219:             if echo is not None and echo not in resp:
220:                 continue
221:             if len(buf) > mark and resp.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
222:                 return True
223:         return False
224: 
225:     def send(x, timeout=60):
226:         b = (x + "\n").encode("latin1")
227:         _mark = len(buf)
228:         n = 0
229:         try:
230:             while n < len(b):
231:                 n += os.write(fd, b[n:])
232:         except OSError:
233:             dead[0] = True
234:             return False
235:         return wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None)
236: 
237:     status, regs, badis = "?", {}, []
238:     #  KEYWORD, and this is not style.  A positional wait(120) silently becomes mark=120 under
239:     #  the pinned parameter order -- a real behaviour change, and the exact mistake the
240:     #  m8820x probe's conversion had to fix in the same commit.
241:     if not wait(timeout=120):
242:         status = "NO-PROMPT"
243:     else:
244:         send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA, 0x11223344))
245:         send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + 4, 0x12345678))
246:         send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + 8, P))
247:         send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + 12, Q))
248:         for off, word, _t, _c in prog:
249:             send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + off, word))
250:         send("pc=0x%08x" % CODE)
251:         status = "SURVIVED"
252:         for off, word, text, _c in prog[:-1]:      # the landing pad is never stepped
253:             mark = len(buf)
254:             if not send("step 1", timeout=30) or dead[0]:
255:                 status = "HOST-DIED"
256:                 break
257:             seg = buf[mark:]
258:             #  Match the emulator's own step line for THIS address and compare the mnemonic
259:             #  it printed against what this table says was planted.
260:             m = re.search(r"%08x:\s+%08x\s+([^\r\n<]+)" % (CODE + off, word), seg)
261:             got = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", m.group(1)).strip() if m else "(no step line)"
262:             if got != text:
263:                 badis.append("0x%02x planted 0x%08x -> %r, expected %r"
264:                              % (off, word, got, text))
265:         if status == "SURVIVED":
266:             mark = len(buf)
267:             if send("reg", timeout=30) and not dead[0]:
268:                 for n in (0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12):
269:                     mm = re.search(r"\b%s\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)"
270:                                    % REGNAME.get(n, "r%d" % n), buf[mark:])
271:                     if mm:
272:                         regs[n] = int(mm.group(1), 16)
273:             else:
274:                 status = "HOST-DIED" if dead[0] else "NO-REG"
275:     #  #539 (pass 2): the SAME teardown as the two pending probes -- quit, DRAIN to EOF (bounded), close
276:     #  the master, then a BOUNDED reap (poll 10 s, kill, wait).  The old sleep-0.3-then-close could SIGHUP a
277:     #  child still shutting down and then block forever in waitpid; and fatals are collected AFTER the
278:     #  drain so a diagnostic printed during shutdown is not missed.
279:     try:
280:         os.write(fd, b"quit\n")
281:     except OSError:
282:         pass
283:     t1 = time.time()
284:     eof = False
285:     while time.time() - t1 < 20.0:
286:         if not rd(0.1):
287:             eof = True
288:             break
289:     fatals = re.findall(r"(\[[^\r\n\]]*footbridge[^\r\n\]]*\])", buf)
290:     try:
291:         os.close(fd)
292:     except OSError:
293:         pass
294:     rpid, st, gone = 0, None, False
295:     t1 = time.time()
296:     while rpid == 0 and time.time() - t1 < 10.0:
297:         try:
298:             rpid, st = os.waitpid(pid, os.WNOHANG)
299:         except OSError:
300:             gone = True      # ECHILD: already reaped elsewhere -- its pid may be recycled; never kill it
301:             break
302:         if rpid == 0:
303:             time.sleep(0.05)
304:     harness_killed = False
305:     if rpid == 0 and not gone:
306:         #  F2 (#539): a kill WE fire is labelled (see the pending probes' close()): 20 s drain + 10 s reap.
307:         harness_killed = True
308:         try:
309:             os.kill(pid, signal.SIGKILL)
310:         except OSError:
311:             pass
312:         try:
313:             _, st = os.waitpid(pid, 0)
314:         except OSError:
315:             st = None
316:     code = os.WEXITSTATUS(st) if st is not None and os.WIFEXITED(st) else None
317:     sig = os.WTERMSIG(st) if st is not None and os.WIFSIGNALED(st) else None
318:     if harness_killed and sig == signal.SIGKILL:
319:         sig = "HARNESS-KILL(%s)" % sig      # our kill, not a product death (a kill that lands on
320:                                              # a zombie reaps the product's own status: unlabelled)
321:     elif not eof and sig is not None:
322:         sig = "HARNESS-CLOSE(%s)" % sig   # the drain deadline expired; our close HUP'd a live child
323:     print("  arm %-36s %-9s exit=%-5s signal=%s" % (label, status, code, sig))
324:     for b in badis:
325:         print("      DISASSEMBLY MISMATCH: %s" % b)
326:     return {"status": status, "regs": regs, "exit": code, "signal": sig,
327:             "fatals": fatals, "badis": badis}

88: /*
89:  *  #442: the counter above was incremented without a bound, so a guest that armed
90:  *  a timer and never acked overflowed it -- and on overflow the int goes negative,
91:  *  DEVICE_TICK's `> 0` is false, and THE EMULATED CLOCK DELIVERS NOTHING FOR 2^31
92:  *  FURTHER TICKS.  Reproduced at rung 3 on an unmodified -E cats: two MMIO writes,
93:  *  then 42.95 s to the wrap and a 40 s dead window, measured three times.
94:  *
95:  *  THE COMPARISON PRECEDES THE INCREMENT, and that is not a style choice.  An
96:  *  increment-then-check form -- which is what the one in-tree precedent for this
97:  *  (dev_luna88k.c:252) actually does -- reintroduces the UB it is meant to remove
98:  *  if the counter ever reaches INT_MAX, because the ++ has already executed by the
99:  *  time the check runs.  luna88k is safe only because its own reset keeps the
100:  *  counter far below INT_MAX; the FORM is still the wrong one to copy.
101:  *
102:  *  Reset to 1 rather than 0, so one interrupt is still owed -- coalescing the debt
103:  *  rather than discarding it.  MEASURED to matter: a reset-to-0 mutant survives the
104:  *  rung-3 drain probe and two of the three offline rows; only the trough row
105:  *  separates them (0 against 1).
106:  *
107:  *  NO MESSAGE FROM HERE.  These run in the SIGALRM handler and debugmsg() is
108:  *  printf underneath, so copying luna88k's diagnostic would put four printf paths
109:  *  inside a signal handler -- and timer.c:274-279 states that nothing in that file
110:  *  prints from the handler.  (luna88k's own callback does, which is a defect there;
111:  *  filed, not fixed here.)  If a diagnostic is ever wanted, latch a flag here and
112:  *  report from DEVICE_TICK, which runs in the main loop.
113:  */
114: static void footbridge_pending(struct footbridge_data *d, int nr)
115: {
116: 	if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] >= d->pending_bound[nr])
117: 		d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] = 1;
118: 	else
119: 		d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] ++;
120: }

378: 	d->irq_status |= interrupt->line;

783: 	 *  BACKWARDS. ***  It called the ceiling "the safe default: it can never
784: 	 *  throttle, only bound."  The ceiling is 68,157,440 -- at cats' 762.94 Hz

168:     (0x14, 0xE3A04A09, "mov r4,#0x9000",        "scratch RAM"),
169:     (0x18, 0xE5945000, "ldr r5,[r4]",           "C1 liveness: plain RAM, expect 0x11223344"),

174:     (0x2c, 0xE3A01080, "mov r1,#0x80",          "TIMER_ENABLE (dc21285reg.h:367)"),

184: #  The free-running loop.  Every word is planted; NONE of it is stepped in arm A.
185: LOOP = [
186:     (0x48, 0xE5808304, "str r8,[r0,#772]",  "outer: TIMER_1_VALUE <- sentinel"),

203: SENT_A = (0xE3A088AB, "mov r8,#0xab0000")     # 0x00AB0000
204: SENT_E = (0xE3A08000, "mov r8,#0")            # arm E: the ONE immediate that differs

210: class Session:
211:     """One emulator process on a cold debugger."""
212: 
213:     def __init__(self):
214:         self.buf = ""
239: 
240:     #  #467: an INACTIVITY deadline, not a total-time one.  This used to read
241:     #  `t = time.time()` once and loop `while time.time() - t < timeout`, so a boot
242:     #  that normally takes 40 s and took 90 s on a LOADED HOST failed -- while bytes
243:     #  were arriving the whole time.  Slow was indistinguishable from stuck, and the
244:     #  battery could only be trusted on an idle machine.
245:     #
246:     #  `rd()` cannot report progress: it returns True both when data arrives and when
247:     #  its select() times out quietly.  So progress is `len(self.buf)` changing.
248:     #
249:     #  STRICTLY MORE PERMISSIVE for a run that produces output, IDENTICAL for one that
250:     #  does not -- it cannot turn a passing run into a failing one.  `timeout` now
251:     #  means SILENCE, which is the thing actually worth failing on.
252:     def wait(self, mark=0, echo=None, timeout=60):
253:         t = time.time()
254:         seen = len(self.buf)
255:         while time.time() - t < timeout:
256:             if not self.rd():
257:                 return False
258:             if len(self.buf) != seen:
259:                 seen = len(self.buf)
260:                 t = time.time()
261:             resp = self.buf[mark:]
262:             if echo is not None and echo not in resp:
263:                 continue
264:             if len(self.buf) > mark and resp.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
265:                 return True
266:         return False
267: 
268:     def send(self, x, timeout=60):
269:         b = (x + "\n").encode("latin1")
270:         _mark = len(self.buf)
271:         n = 0
272:         try:
273:             while n < len(b):
274:                 n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
275:         except OSError:
276:             self.dead = True
277:             return False
278:         return self.wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None, timeout=timeout)
279: 
280:     def free_run(self, seconds):
281:         """`continue`, drain for `seconds`, then SIGINT back to the prompt.
282: 
283:         Returns (ok, measured_free_run_seconds).  The pty is DRAINED throughout: a full
284:         pty buffer blocks the emulator, which would look like a device that stopped
285:         ticking -- the exact conclusion this probe is trying to reach, so it must not be
286:         manufacturable by the harness."""
287:         mark = len(self.buf)
288:         b, n = b"continue\n", 0
289:         try:
290:             while n < len(b):
291:                 n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
292:         except OSError:
293:             self.dead = True
294:             return False, 0.0
295:         t0 = time.time()
296:         while time.time() - t0 < seconds:
297:             if not self.rd(0.25):
298:                 return False, time.time() - t0
299:         try:
300:             os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGINT)
301:         except OSError:
302:             self.dead = True
303:             return False, time.time() - t0
304:         ok = self.wait(mark=mark, timeout=20)
305:         t1 = time.time()
306:         if not ok and not self.dead:
307:             try:
308:                 os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGINT)
309:             except OSError:
310:                 pass
311:             ok = self.wait(mark=mark, timeout=20)
330:         vals = [out.get(addr + 4 * i) for i in range(n)]
331:         return None if any(v is None for v in vals) else vals
332: 
333:     def regs(self, which=(0, 1, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9)):
334:         mark = len(self.buf)
335:         if not self.send("reg", timeout=30) or self.dead:
336:             return {}
337:         seg = self.buf[mark:]
338:         names = {10: "sl", 11: "fp", 12: "ip", 13: "sp", 14: "lr"}
339:         got = {}
340:         for k in which:
341:             nm = names.get(k, "r%d" % k)
342:             m = re.search(r"\b%s\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)" % nm, seg)
343:             if m:
344:                 got[k] = int(m.group(1), 16)
345:         return got
346: 
347:     def close(self, patience=20.0):
348:         try:
349:             os.write(self.fd, b"quit\n")
350:         except OSError:
351:             pass
352:         # Drain through EOF; closing a live master can SIGHUP the child.
353:         deadline = time.monotonic() + patience
354:         eof = False
355:         while True:
356:             left = deadline - time.monotonic()
357:             if left <= 0:
358:                 break
359:             if not self.rd(min(0.1, left)):
360:                 eof = True
361:                 break
362:         try:
363:             os.close(self.fd)
364:         except OSError:
365:             pass
366:         #  #539 (pass 2): BOUNDED reap.  A child that survives the master close (SIGHUP ignored or
367:         #  blocked, or stopped) would hang a blocking waitpid forever; poll, then kill, then wait.
368:         pid, st, gone = 0, None, False
369:         t1 = time.time()
370:         while pid == 0 and time.time() - t1 < 10.0:
371:             try:
372:                 pid, st = os.waitpid(self.pid, os.WNOHANG)
373:             except OSError:
374:                 gone = True      # ECHILD: already reaped elsewhere -- its pid may be recycled; never kill it
375:                 break
376:             if pid == 0:
377:                 time.sleep(0.05)
378:         harness_killed = False
379:         if pid == 0 and not gone:
380:             #  F2 (#539): a kill WE fire is labelled, so a red from it is attributed to the harness --
381:             #  a healthy child SIGKILLed by a too-short budget would otherwise read like a product
382:             #  signal death (byte-identical to the y_sigkill mutant).  Budgets: 20 s drain + 10 s here.
383:             harness_killed = True
384:             try:
385:                 os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGKILL)
386:             except OSError:
387:                 pass
388:             try:
389:                 _, st = os.waitpid(self.pid, 0)
390:             except OSError:
391:                 st = None
392:         code = os.WEXITSTATUS(st) if st is not None and os.WIFEXITED(st) else None
393:         sig = os.WTERMSIG(st) if st is not None and os.WIFSIGNALED(st) else None
394:         if harness_killed and sig == signal.SIGKILL:
395:             sig = "HARNESS-KILL(%s)" % sig     # our kill; a kill landing on a zombie reaps the
396:                                                 # product's own status and stays unlabelled
397:         elif not eof and sig is not None:
398:             sig = "HARNESS-CLOSE(%s)" % sig   # the drain deadline expired; our close() HUP'd a live child
399:         return dict(exit=code, signal=sig)
412: def step_and_check(s, prog, n):
413:     """`step 1` n times; check every EXECUTED word against what was planted at that
414:     address AND its disassembly against the emulator's own spelling.  Returns
415:     (mismatches, covered_addresses, executed)."""
416:     planted = {CODE + off: (word, text) for off, word, text, _ in prog}
417:     bad, covered, executed = [], set(), []
418:     for _ in range(n):
419:         mark = len(s.buf)
420:         if not s.send("step 1", timeout=30) or s.dead:
421:             bad.append("host died or no prompt during step %d" % (len(executed) + 1))
422:             break
423:         seg = s.buf[mark:]
424:         m = re.search(r"([0-9a-f]{8}):\s+([0-9a-f]{8})\s+([^\r\n<]+)", seg)
425:         if not m:
426:             bad.append("no step line at step %d" % (len(executed) + 1))
427:             break
428:         addr, word = int(m.group(1), 16), int(m.group(2), 16)
429:         got = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", m.group(3)).strip()
430:         executed.append((addr, word, got))
431:         if addr not in planted:
432:             bad.append("stepped 0x%08x, which was never planted" % addr)
433:             continue
434:         pw, pt = planted[addr]
435:         covered.add(addr)
436:         if word != pw:
437:             bad.append("0x%08x read back 0x%08x, planted 0x%08x" % (addr, word, pw))
438:         if pt is not None and got != pt:
439:             bad.append("0x%08x 0x%08x -> %r, expected %r" % (addr, word, got, pt))
440:     return bad, covered, executed

477:     prog = MMU_OFF + prologue(*SENT_A) + [KILL, (0x4c, NOP, NOPTXT, "")]
478:     s = Session()

481:     plant(s, prog)

483:     died = s.dead
484:     if not died:
485:         #  The exit may land between the write and our read; one more poke settles it.
486:         s.send("reg", timeout=10)
487:         died = s.dead
488:     end = s.close()
489:     seen = "ENABLE CLEAR" in s.buf
490:     return dict(status="HOST-DIED" if died else "SURVIVED", saw_fatal=seen,
491:                 badis=bad, nsteps=len(ex), **end)

597:     def row(tag, good, detail):
598:         nonlocal ok
599:         ok &= bool(good)
600:         print("  %-4s %-47s %s" % ("ok" if good else "FAIL", tag, detail))

285: # ------------------------------------------------------------------ session
286: class Session:
287:     def __init__(self):
288:         self.buf, self.dead = "", False
289:         self.pid, self.fd = pty.fork()
290:         if self.pid == 0:
291:             os.execvp(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-T", "-E", "cats", "-M", "32",
292:                             "0x%x:%s" % (CODE, STUB)])
293:             os._exit(127)
294: 
295:     def rd(self, t=0.4):
296:         r, _, _ = select.select([self.fd], [], [], t)
297:         if self.fd not in r:
298:             return True
299:         try:
300:             d = os.read(self.fd, 65536)
301:         except OSError:
302:             self.dead = True
303:             return False
304:         if not d:
305:             self.dead = True
306:             return False
307:         self.buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
308:         return True
309: 
310:     #  #467: an INACTIVITY deadline, not a total-time one.  This used to read
311:     #  `t = time.time()` once and loop `while time.time() - t < timeout`, so a boot
312:     #  that normally takes 40 s and took 90 s on a LOADED HOST failed -- while bytes
313:     #  were arriving the whole time.  Slow was indistinguishable from stuck, and the
314:     #  battery could only be trusted on an idle machine.
315:     #
316:     #  `rd()` cannot report progress: it returns True both when data arrives and when
317:     #  its select() times out quietly.  So progress is `len(self.buf)` changing.
318:     #
319:     #  STRICTLY MORE PERMISSIVE for a run that produces output, IDENTICAL for one that
320:     #  does not -- it cannot turn a passing run into a failing one.  `timeout` now
321:     #  means SILENCE, which is the thing actually worth failing on.
322:     def wait(self, mark=0, echo=None, timeout=60):
323:         t = time.time()
324:         seen = len(self.buf)
325:         while time.time() - t < timeout:
326:             if not self.rd():
327:                 return False
328:             if len(self.buf) != seen:
329:                 seen = len(self.buf)
330:                 t = time.time()
331:             resp = self.buf[mark:]
332:             if echo is not None and echo not in resp:
333:                 continue
334:             if len(self.buf) > mark and resp.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
335:                 return True
336:         return False
337: 
338:     def send(self, x, timeout=60):
339:         b = (x + "\n").encode("latin1")
340:         #  `_mark`, not `mark`: gate 6's fresh-mark census keys on that exact spelling.
341:         #  Every other pty probe in the tree writes it that way, so a probe spelling it
342:         #  `mark` has the guard PRESENT and UNCOUNTED -- two of the three counts move and
343:         #  the third does not, a signature that reads like a defect and is not one.
344:         _mark, n = len(self.buf), 0
345:         try:
346:             while n < len(b):
347:                 n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
348:         except OSError:
349:             self.dead = True
350:             return False
351:         return self.wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None, timeout=timeout)
352: 
353:     def free_run(self, seconds):
382:     def read_words(self, addr, n):
383:         mark = len(self.buf)
384:         if not self.send("dump 0x%08x 0x%08x" % (addr, addr + 4 * n)):
385:             return None
386:         out = {}
387:         for line in self.buf[mark:].splitlines():
388:             m = re.match(r"\s*0x([0-9a-f]{8})\s+((?:[0-9a-f]{8}\s+){1,4})", line)
389:             if not m:
390:                 continue
391:             base = int(m.group(1), 16)
392:             for i, w in enumerate(m.group(2).split()):
393:                 out[base + 4 * i] = struct.unpack("<I", struct.pack(">I", int(w, 16)))[0]
394:         vals = [out.get(addr + 4 * i) for i in range(n)]
395:         return None if any(v is None for v in vals) else vals
396: 
397:     def regs(self, which=(3, 5, 6, 7)):
398:         mark = len(self.buf)
399:         if not self.send("reg", timeout=30) or self.dead:
400:             return {}
401:         seg, got = self.buf[mark:], {}
402:         for k in which:
403:             m = re.search(r"\br%d\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)" % k, seg)
404:             if m:
405:                 got[k] = int(m.group(1), 16)
406:         return got

408:     def close(self, patience=20.0):
409:         try:
410:             os.write(self.fd, b"quit\n")
411:         except OSError:
412:             pass
413:         # Drain through EOF; closing a live master can SIGHUP the child.
414:         deadline = time.monotonic() + patience
415:         eof = False
416:         while True:
417:             left = deadline - time.monotonic()
418:             if left <= 0:
419:                 break
420:             if not self.rd(min(0.1, left)):
421:                 eof = True
422:                 break
423:         try:
424:             os.close(self.fd)
425:         except OSError:
426:             pass
427:         #  #539 (pass 2): BOUNDED reap.  A child that survives the master close (SIGHUP ignored or
428:         #  blocked, or stopped) would hang a blocking waitpid forever; poll, then kill, then wait.
429:         pid, st, gone = 0, None, False
430:         t1 = time.time()
431:         while pid == 0 and time.time() - t1 < 10.0:
432:             try:
433:                 pid, st = os.waitpid(self.pid, os.WNOHANG)
434:             except OSError:
435:                 gone = True      # ECHILD: already reaped elsewhere -- its pid may be recycled; never kill it
436:                 break
437:             if pid == 0:
438:                 time.sleep(0.05)
439:         harness_killed = False
440:         if pid == 0 and not gone:
441:             #  F2 (#539): a kill WE fire is labelled, so a red from it is attributed to the harness --
442:             #  a healthy child SIGKILLed by a too-short budget would otherwise read like a product
443:             #  signal death (byte-identical to the y_sigkill mutant).  Budgets: 20 s drain + 10 s here.
444:             harness_killed = True
445:             try:
446:                 os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGKILL)
447:             except OSError:
448:                 pass
449:             try:
450:                 _, st = os.waitpid(self.pid, 0)
451:             except OSError:
452:                 st = None
453:         code = os.WEXITSTATUS(st) if st is not None and os.WIFEXITED(st) else None
454:         sig = os.WTERMSIG(st) if st is not None and os.WIFSIGNALED(st) else None
455:         if harness_killed and sig == signal.SIGKILL:
456:             sig = "HARNESS-KILL(%s)" % sig     # our kill; a kill landing on a zombie reaps the
457:                                                 # product's own status and stays unlabelled
458:         elif not eof and sig is not None:
459:             sig = "HARNESS-CLOSE(%s)" % sig   # the drain deadline expired; our close() HUP'd a live child
460:         return dict(exit=code, signal=sig)
489: def step_and_check(s, prog, n):
490:     planted = {CODE + off: (w, t) for off, w, t, _ in prog}
491:     bad, covered, ex = [], set(), []
492:     for _ in range(n):
493:         mark = len(s.buf)
494:         if not s.send("step 1", timeout=30) or s.dead:
495:             bad.append("host died / no prompt at step %d" % (len(ex) + 1))
496:             break
497:         m = re.search(r"([0-9a-f]{8}):\s+([0-9a-f]{8})\s+([^\r\n<]+)", s.buf[mark:])
498:         if not m:
499:             bad.append("no step line at step %d" % (len(ex) + 1))
500:             break
501:         addr, word = int(m.group(1), 16), int(m.group(2), 16)
502:         got = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", m.group(3)).strip()
503:         ex.append((addr, word, got))
504:         if addr not in planted:
505:             bad.append("stepped 0x%08x, never planted" % addr)
506:             continue
507:         pw, pt = planted[addr]
508:         covered.add(addr)
509:         if word != pw:
510:             bad.append("0x%08x read back 0x%08x, planted 0x%08x" % (addr, word, pw))
511:         if pt is not None and not spell_ok(pt, got):
512:             bad.append("0x%08x %08x -> %r, expected %r" % (addr, word, got, pt))
513:     return bad, covered, ex
514: 
515: 
516: def run_arm(label, mmu_on=False, disable=False, accrue=None, max_drain_s=30.0):
517:     accrue = ACCRUE_S if accrue is None else accrue
518:     prog = build(mmu_on=mmu_on, disable_during_accrual=disable)
519:     s = Session()
520:     if not s.wait(timeout=120):
521:         return dict(label=label, status="NO-PROMPT", **s.close())
522:     plant(s, prog)
600: def run_kill():
601:     prog = build(kill=True)
602:     s = Session()
603:     if not s.wait(timeout=120):
604:         return dict(status="NO-PROMPT", saw=False, **s.close())
605:     plant(s, prog)
606:     step_and_check(s, prog, PROLOGUE_N + 3)
607:     died = s.dead
608:     if not died:
609:         s.send("reg", timeout=10)
610:         died = s.dead
611:     end = s.close()
612:     saw = "ENABLE CLEAR" in s.buf
613:     return dict(status="HOST-DIED" if died else "SURVIVED", saw=saw, **end)
614:

616: def main():
675:     ok = True
676: 
677:     def row(tag, good, detail):
678:         nonlocal ok
679:         ok &= bool(good)
680:         print("  %-4s %-46s %s" % ("ok" if good else "FAIL", tag, detail))
681: 
682:     def survived(a):
683:         return a["status"] == "SURVIVED" and a.get("exit") == 0
684:

696:     row("C2 the MMU is OFF in the device arms",

699:     row("A1 device signature VENDOR_ID",

701:         "D r6=0x%x X r6=0x%x (expect 0x%x)" % (rD.get(6, -1), rX.get(6, -1), VENDOR))
702:     row("A2 device signature: the handler's own 24-bit mask",

705:     drain_words = {CODE + o for o in range(0x70, 0xe8, 4)}

711:            " ".join("0x%x" % a for a in sorted(drain_words - E["covered"])) or "none"))

714:         and survived(D),

717:     row("T  the MMU-ON control cannot see the device",
718:         #  F2 (#539): the keys must be PRESENT -- None != VENDOR is True, so a register-parse miss
719:         #  greened this row once survived() forbade the halt outcome.

 succeeded in 599ms:
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """#432: the DC21285 "footbridge" driven by REAL GUEST INSTRUCTIONS, with no rig.
3: 
4: WHY THIS EXISTS ALONGSIDE regress/diff_footbridge.c.  The offline differential calls
5: footbridge_effective_cycles() and dev_footbridge_access() directly, so it proves BEHAVIOUR
6: but cannot prove the device is REACHABLE by a guest -- it would stay green under a change
7: that unmapped it (a memory_device_register length, a DM_ flag, or machine_cats.c's
8: `addr=0x42000000` line).  Until this probe, R6 was THE ONLY CODE ROUND IN THIS TREE WITH NO
9: REACHABILITY WITNESS OF ANY KIND: no rig boots a footbridge and there is no m8online analog.
10: This is that analog.  It goes through the real address decode and the real memory_rw
11: plumbing, on a COLD DEBUGGER -- no boot, no disk image, no guest OS.
12: 
13: *** THE TRAP THIS PROBE EXISTS TO SURVIVE, and it is the whole reason for arm T. ***
14: machine_cats.c:131 calls arm_setup_initial_translation_table(), which (cpu_arm.c:239-255)
15: writes a section descriptor for i in 0..255 at VA (j<<28)+(i<<20) -> PA i<<20 -- i.e. the
16: TOP NIBBLE OF THE VA IS IGNORED and only the low 256 MB of physical space is mapped
17: anywhere.  The footbridge sits at PA 0x42000000, outside that window, so under the boot-time
18: MMU it is UNREACHABLE: VA 0x42000000 aliases to PA 0x02000000 and a load returns 0.
19: 
20: That is not a hypothetical.  The first version of this probe returned 0x0 on every device
21: row WITH ITS RAM CONTROL STILL GREEN, and 0x0 is exactly what "the device is not there"
22: looks like -- so a survival-shaped or absence-shaped assertion would have CONFIRMED THE
23: OPPOSITE CONCLUSION.  A RAM liveness row cannot see it, because VA 0x9000 maps to PA 0x9000
24: identically with the MMU on or off.
25: 
26: So the guard is not a comment, it is arm T: THE SAME PROGRAM WITH THE THREE MMU-DISABLE
27: INSTRUCTIONS REPLACED BY NOPS MUST NOT PRODUCE THE DEVICE'S ANSWER.  If arm T ever reads
28: 0x1011, the discriminator is dead and every other row here is meaningless -- and it goes RED
29: rather than silently green.  Arm L additionally reads CP15 c1 BACK after the mcr and asserts
30: bit 0 is clear, so the precondition is MEASURED rather than inferred from the store having
31: been issued.  Three separate statements, deliberately: the guest turned the MMU off, the two
32: arms really differ in MMU state, and only the MMU-off arm can see the device.
33: 
34: VALUES, NOT SURVIVAL (rounds 79/80, and m8820x_sites_probe.py's header says the same).
35: A row that only asks "did the host stay up" cannot tell a reachable device from an absent
36: one -- an unmapped read survives too, it just returns 0.  So the two device rows assert
37: values that ONLY dev_footbridge.c can produce:
38:   * A1 reads VENDOR_ID and requires 0x1011 (dev_footbridge.c `case VENDOR_ID`, the
39:     DC21285_VENDOR_ID assignment).
40:     Nonzero and specific: the hand-assembled-encoding trap this project has been bitten by
41:     yields 0 from a wrong register field, and 0 is the one answer this row rejects.
42:   * A2 writes 0x12345678 to TIMER_1_LOAD and reads back 0x00345678.  THE VANISHING TOP BYTE
43:     IS THE HANDLER'S OWN SIGNATURE: `case TIMER_1_LOAD`'s write branch (one case serves all four
44:     timers through the fold) stores `idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL`
45:     (0x00FFFFFF, dc21285reg.h:371).  RAM, or any decode that merely absorbed the store,
46:     would return 0x12345678 unchanged.  This row therefore distinguishes "the device
47:     handled it" from "the write landed somewhere", which A1 alone cannot.
48: 
49: THE KILL PAIR, which is this probe's own failability control.  Arms L and K differ by
50: EXACTLY ONE INSTRUCTION.  Arm K adds a guest read of IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR (base+0x18c), which
51: dev_footbridge.c's `case IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR` read branch answers with fatal() then exit(1) --
52: an UNFIXED site, used here rather than repaired, because a probe that reports SURVIVED for
53: every row is vacuous unless something in it can still die.  Arm L must live; arm K must
54: exit 1 and say so.  If both survived, the guest instructions are not reaching the device at
55: all and every SURVIVED verdict above is worthless; if both died, the probe is killing the
56: host for its own reasons.  The one-instruction delta is the entire argument.
57:   #539 moved this arm from +0x188 to +0x18c.  +0x188 (IRQEnable) is a DOCUMENTED READ-ONLY
58:   register (DC21285 Table 7-10; 7.3.31: "can only be changed by writing to the EnableSet and
59:   EnableClear registers", reset 0) and now answers reads -- rows E0-E6 below.  +0x18c is
60:   write-only per 7.3.33 (IRQEnableClear), so a kill there is a decline-class site still dying,
61:   and the same deletion spilled onto the CLEAR read branch -- with or without its diagnostic --
62:   makes K survive, so K1 reds on the exit status it requires.
63:   The bridge is TEMPORARY: the round that declines +0x18c retires it and builds the harness
64:   legs (startup rejection + mid-session quit, status reaped) in the same commit.
65: 
66: ARM E -- #539's ACCEPT rows, in a FOURTH arm so that arms L/T/K are byte-identical to what
67: they were.  E carries its own C1/A1 (E0 = 0 is also what an ABSENT device returns, so the
68: E rows are meaningless unless this arm demonstrably reached the device) and then:
69:   E0 read IRQEnable before any write            -> 0            (reset value, 7.3.31)
70:   E1 write P=0x2002 to Set, read               -> P            (readable; catches a bit
71:                                                                   swap, a constant, a wrong
72:                                                                   field, a masked value)
73:   E2 write Q=0x60000000 to Set, read           -> P|Q          (pins |=; bit 30 set so a
74:                                                                   diagnostic kept for the top
75:                                                                   bits fires here)
76:   E3 ldrb  +0x188                              -> 0x02         (low byte: len 1 reaches the
77:   E6 ldrh  +0x188                              -> 0x2002        same case; the CSR drives
78:                                                                   D[31:0] and the SA-110 takes
79:                                                                   the lane, DC21285 3.2.x)
80:   E5 write P to Clear, read Set                -> Q            (Clear works; = the E2 value
81:                                                                   with P removed)
82:   E4 no BRACKETED diagnostic naming footbridge anywhere in arm E -- the `fatals` regex is
83:      `[ ... footbridge ... ]`, the house diagnostic shape; a kept fatal() with its exit gone
84:      is caught; a diagnostic rewritten WITHOUT brackets or without the device name is NOT
85:      (listed under NOT COVERED below)
86: Execution order is E0,E1,E2,E3,E6,E5: E6 wants 0x2002, which is true only while P is set.
87: P and Q are loaded from RAM (DATA+8, DATA+12), like 0x12345678: P is not a rotated ARM
88: immediate (bits 1 and 13); Q is (0x06 ROR 4) and is loaded the same way for uniformity.
89: Values land in r7,r1,r2,r9,fp,ip; r1/r2 are A2's registers in arm L and are free here
90: because arm E does not score A2.  TESTED PATTERNS, stated so the contract is checkable:
91: P = bits 1,13 and Q = bits 29,30 only; bit 31 and every other bit are untested by value.
92: 
93: CONTRACT (what this detector is designed to catch, and what it is not).  On the SET read
94: branch: the exit reinstated (E0 dies), reinstated only when irq_enable == 0 (E0), a wrong
95: field/constant/mask/bit-swap (E1, E2), a width guard (E3, E6), a kept diagnostic (E4), a
96: diagnostic kept for bit 30 (E4; E2 if it exits -- bit 31 is never set here, so a bit-31-only
97: guard is UNTESTED).  On the CLEAR arms: a broken `&= ~`
98: (E5), the exit removed from the CLEAR read too (K1).  NOT COVERED: (a) no row observes the
99: CPU IRQ line -- an edit to the write arm's assert condition (`irq_status | irq_enable`) is
100: invisible to every row here, because cpu_dyntrans.c suppresses interrupt delivery under
101: `step` and CPSR I/F stay set; it needs a free-running arm with a positive delivery control,
102: filed with IRQSoft; (b) +0x189..+0x18b: real hardware selects the same CSR by A[10:2] and
103: returns lanes 1-3, the model routes them to `default:` and returns 0 -- a device-wide decode
104: deviation, filed, not rowed; (c) irq_status and fiq_enable are 0 throughout, so a mutant
105: returning either at +0x188 is caught by E1/E2 only through the value, not distinguished
106: from a constant 0.  (d) D1 compares the disassembly of every STEPPED word; the landing pad is
107: planted and never stepped, and a step that ends the process is not compared.  (e) E4 sees only
108: a BRACKETED diagnostic that names footbridge (the `fatals` regex); a bare-text diagnostic on
109: the read path escapes it.
110: TEARDOWN CONVENTION this file's bounded drain rests on: rd() returns True on a select TIMEOUT
111: and False only on EOF/EIO, so the post-quit drain loop breaks on EOF or its deadline, never on
112: a quiet interval (the same convention as the two pending probes' Session.rd).
113: 
114: EVERY PLANTED WORD IS CHECKED AGAINST THE DISASSEMBLY THE EMULATOR PRINTS AS IT STEPS IT --
115: not against a separate `unassemble`, because the step line is the instruction that actually
116: executed.  This project has a recorded incident where a wrong register field made a gate row
117: measure the wrong thing for months.
118: 
119: COST, measured: see PROBE_WALL below -- 4 cold-debugger launches, no boot.  A booting-rig row
120: was deliberately NOT written: no rig in this tree boots a footbridge machine at all, which is
121: the gap this probe closes, and adding one would put a load-sensitive wall-clock oracle into a
122: battery already carrying ~37.

123: /*
124:  *  machine_destroy():
125:  *
126:  *  Destroys a machine object.
127:  */
128: void machine_destroy(struct machine *machine)
129: {
130: 	int i;
131: 
132: 	for (i=0; i<machine->ncpus; i++)
133: 		cpu_destroy(machine->cpus[i]);
134: 
135: 	if (machine->name != NULL)
136: 	 	free(machine->name);
137: 
138: 	if (machine->path != NULL)
139: 		free(machine->path);
140: 
141: 	/*  Remove any remaining level-1 settings:  */
142: 	settings_remove_all(machine->settings);
143: 	settings_destroy(machine->settings);
144: 
145: 	free(machine);
146: }
147: 
148: 
149: /*
150:  *  machine_name_to_type():
151:  *
152:  *  Take a type and a subtype as strings, and convert them into numeric
153:  *  values used internally throughout the code.
154:  *
155:  *  Return value is 1 on success, 0 if there was no match.
156:  *  Also, any errors/warnings are printed using fatal()/debug().
157:  */
158: int machine_name_to_type(char *stype, char *ssubtype,
159: 	int *type, int *subtype)
160: {
161: 	struct machine_entry *me;
162: 	int i, j, k, nmatches = 0;
163: 
164: 	*type = MACHINE_NONE;
165: 	*subtype = 0;
166: 
167: 	/*  Check stype, and optionally ssubtype:  */
168: 	me = first_machine_entry;
169: 	while (me != NULL) {
170: 		for (i=0; i<me->n_aliases; i++)
171: 			if (strcasecmp(me->aliases[i], stype) == 0) {
172: 				/*  Found a type:  */
173: 				*type = me->machine_type;
174: 
175: 				if (me->n_subtypes == 0)
176: 					return 1;
177: 
178: 				/*  Check for subtype:  */
179: 				for (j=0; j<me->n_subtypes; j++)
180: 					for (k=0; k<me->subtype[j]->n_aliases;
181: 					    k++)
182: 						if (strcasecmp(ssubtype,
183: 						    me->subtype[j]->aliases[k]
184: 						    ) == 0) {
185: 							*subtype = me->subtype[
186: 							    j]->machine_subtype;
187: 							return 1;
188: 						}
189: 
190: 				fatal("Unknown subtype '%s' for emulation"
191: 				    " '%s'\n", ssubtype, stype);
192: 				if (!ssubtype[0])
193: 					fatal("(Maybe you forgot the -e"
194: 					    " command line option?)\n");
195: 
196: 				/*  List the available subtypes, instead of
197: 				    just complaining (see the TODO list):  */
198: 				fatal("\nAvailable subtypes for '%s' are:\n",
199: 				    stype);
200: 				for (j=0; j<me->n_subtypes; j++) {
201: 					struct machine_entry_subtype *mes =
202: 					    me->subtype[j];
203: 					fatal("  %s (", mes->name);
204: 					for (k=0; k<mes->n_aliases; k++)
205: 						fatal("%s\"%s\"", k? ", " : "",
206: 						    mes->aliases[k]);
207: 					fatal(")\n");
208: 				}
209: 				fatal("\nUse an alias when selecting a "
210: 				    "subtype, e.g.:  -e %s\n",
211: 				    me->subtype[0]->aliases[0]);
212: 				exit(1);
213: 			}
214: 
215: 		me = me->next;
216: 	}
217: 
218: 	/*  Not found? Then just check ssubtype:  */
219: 	me = first_machine_entry;
220: 	while (me != NULL) {
221: 		if (me->n_subtypes == 0) {
222: 			me = me->next;
223: 			continue;
224: 		}
225:

		color_prompt();
		printf("-E xx");
		color_normal();

	}

	if (emul->machines[0]->machine_type == MACHINE_NONE) {
		printf("No machine type specified?\nRun  ");
		color_prompt();
		printf("gxemul -H");
		color_normal();
		printf("  for a list of available machine types.\n"
		    "Then use the ");
		color_prompt();
		printf("-e");

		printf(" or ");
		color_prompt();
		printf("-E");
		color_normal();
		printf(" option(s) to specify the machine type.\n");
		return 1;
	}

	device_set_exit_on_error(0);
	console_warn_if_slaves_are_needed(1);

	// Print "INFO" during startup, but then just "WARNINGS" by default.
	debugmsg_add_verbosity_level(SUBSYS_ALL, -1);


	/*  Run the emulation:  */
	emul_run(emul);


	/*
	 *  Deinitialize everything:
	 */

	console_deinit();

	emul_destroy(emul);

	settings_remove_all(global_settings);
	settings_destroy(global_settings);

	return 0;
}

210: class Session:
211:     """One emulator process on a cold debugger."""
212: 
213:     def __init__(self):
214:         self.buf = ""
215:         self.dead = False
216:         self.pid, self.fd = pty.fork()
217:         if self.pid == 0:
218:             #  EXPLICIT PATH.  os.execvp on a bare name searches PATH; every arm then
219:             #  reports dead, which looks exactly like a wedged session rather than a
220:             #  bad invocation.
221:             os.execvp(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-T", "-E", "cats", "-M", "32",
222:                             "0x%x:%s" % (CODE, STUB)])
223:             os._exit(127)
224: 
225:     def rd(self, t=0.4):
226:         r, _, _ = select.select([self.fd], [], [], t)
227:         if self.fd not in r:
228:             return True
229:         try:
230:             d = os.read(self.fd, 65536)
231:         except OSError:
232:             self.dead = True
233:             return False
234:         if not d:
235:             self.dead = True
236:             return False
237:         self.buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
238:         return True

codex
The footbridge source and all three probes are unchanged from `9c28ca5`. Two premises need correction: this file has no diagnostic latch fields to imitate, and FIQ enable cannot be read back here—its read arm returns the default zero. The new rows therefore need a stated way to observe FIQ state preservation. I also found that the sites probe’s `send()` drops its timeout argument; the other two launchers forward it.
