355: 
356: /*
357:  *  debug() and fatal():
358:  *
359:  *  These are here because of all the legacy code that uses them. Basically,
360:  *  debug() is mapped to something that is only displayed with verbosity
361:  *  at the default level, and fatal() is used to always print stuff. With some
362:  *  hacks to make it work reasonable with -q and when executing normally or
363:  *  when executing single-stepping.
364:  */
365: void debug(const char *fmt, ...)
366: {
367: 	va_list argp;
368: 	bool ss = single_step || about_to_enter_single_step;
369: 	int v = verbose;
370: 	if (emul_executing)
371: 		v--;
372: 	if (ss)
373: 		v++;
374: 
375: 	if ((quiet_mode && !ss) || v < 0)
376: 		return;
377: 
378: 	va_start(argp, fmt);
379: 	va_debug(argp, fmt);
380: 	va_end(argp);
381: }
382: 
383: 
384: void fatal(const char *fmt, ...)
385: {
386: 	va_list argp;
387: 
388: 	va_start(argp, fmt);
389: 	va_debug(argp, fmt);
390: 	va_end(argp);
391: }
392: 
393: 
394: void debugmsg_set_verbosity_level(int subsystem, int verbosity)
395: {
396: 	if (subsystem != SUBSYS_ALL)
397: 		debugmsg_current_verbosity[subsystem] = verbosity;
398: 	else
399: 		for (size_t i = 0; i < debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems; ++i)
400: 			debugmsg_current_verbosity[i] = verbosity;
401: }
402: 
403: 
404: void debugmsg_add_verbosity_level(int subsystem, int verbosity_delta)
405: {
406: 	if (subsystem != SUBSYS_ALL)
407: 		debugmsg_current_verbosity[subsystem] += verbosity_delta;
408: 	else
409: 		for (size_t i = 0; i < debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems; ++i)
410: 			debugmsg_current_verbosity[i] += verbosity_delta;
411: 
412: 	for (size_t i = 0; i < debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems; ++i)
413: 		if (debugmsg_current_verbosity[i] < 0)
414: 			debugmsg_current_verbosity[i] = 0;
415: }

The zero-state rows miss constant-zero status reads and interrupt-output changes; per-site fresh processes also miss a shared latch. For K2, observing failure after `close()` would be circular: cleanup sends `quit`, and a later `send()` can report `dead` merely because the file descriptor is closed.  succeeded in 233ms:
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """Offline check: does `wait()` fail on SILENCE, or merely on ELAPSED TIME?
3: 
4: WHY THIS EXISTS.  Every pty probe in this tree waited on an ABSOLUTE deadline --
5: `t = time.time()` once, then `while time.time() - t < timeout`.  That budget is
6: spent by the CLOCK, not by the guest, so a boot that normally takes 40 s and takes
7: 90 s on a loaded host FAILS -- while bytes were arriving steadily the whole time.
8: A run that is merely SLOW was indistinguishable from one that is STUCK, and the
9: battery could only be trusted on an idle machine.
10: 
11: #467 makes the deadline an INACTIVITY deadline: the clock resets whenever the
12: buffer grows.  `timeout` then means SILENCE, which is the thing actually worth
13: failing on.
14: 
15: WHAT A PRINT CANNOT PROVE.  Reading the converted source tells you the assignment
16: is there; it does not tell you the LOOP honours it.  This file proves the
17: behaviour instead, against a stub in place of the emulator -- no rig, no build, no
18: wall-clock oracle of its own, so it is as load-insensitive as the property it
19: defends.
20: 
21: THE STRADDLE.  Two legs, and BOTH must hold or the conversion is worthless:
22: 
23:   SLOW-BUT-PROGRESSING   a stream that emits steadily and finishes well past the
24:                          deadline.  The OLD shape FAILS it -- that IS the defect
25:                          -- and the NEW shape must PASS.
26:   GENUINELY STUCK        a stream that emits nothing at all.  BOTH shapes must
27:                          FAIL.  This leg is the one that matters: a conversion
28:                          that made the timeout unreachable would sail through the
29:                          first leg and defend nothing.
30: 
31: The two legs are deliberately opposite in sign.  A change that passes only the
32: first has not fixed the timeout, it has REMOVED it.
33: 
34: NOT COVERED, and deliberately: `free_run(seconds)` is NOT converted anywhere.  It
35: is not a timeout -- it is an EXPOSURE WINDOW that runs the guest for a set period
36: and then SIGINTs.  Converting it would change the experiment rather than protect
37: it, and a naive sweep over `time.time()` sites would have eaten it.

VERDICT: REVISE. The survivable device behavior is reasonable, but the proposed rows do not yet prove state preservation, latch independence, or the durable harness controls.

The checkout reports HEAD **`f685d75`**, not `9c28ca5`. The inspected footbridge source and three probes are unchanged against `9c28ca5`; `dev_footbridge.c` has 899 lines and matches your quoted line numbers.

**Q1(a): The register directions are correct; the semantic justification needs qualification.** I checked the named datasheet and the fork’s register definitions.

| Declined access | Datasheet evidence |
|---|---|
| WRITE IRQ_STATUS, `+0x180` | §7.3.29: “This read-only register is a bit-wise AND of RawStatus and IRQEnable/FIQEnable.” |
| WRITE IRQ_RAW_STATUS, `+0x184` | §7.3.30: “The IRQRawStatus and FIQRawStatus read-only registers always contain identical data.” |
| READ IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, `+0x18c` | §7.3.33: “This write-only register is used to clear bits in the IRQEnable/FIQEnable register.” |
| WRITE FIQ_STATUS, `+0x280` | §7.3.29, the same read-only status definition. |
| WRITE FIQ_RAW_STATUS, `+0x284` | §7.3.30, the same read-only raw-status definition. |

[Datasheet register descriptions](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/dc21285.txt:6886).

Two qualifications matter:

- Table 7-1’s access legend says **“Read only. Writes have no effect.”** It appears under §7.1 and supplies additional support for ignoring RO writes. “No write-side register in Table 7-10” is more precise than treating their effects as wholly undefined.
- §3.1.2 specifies zero for a **nonexistent CSR**. It does not explicitly equate reading an existing write-only address with selecting a nonexistent CSR. Returning zero at `+0x18c` is a defensible, adopted emulator policy; the quoted passage is not a direct specification of IRQEnableClear read data.

I support member 3 as a bounded preservation of existing diagnostics. Member 2 is also defensible at every site. Neither retaining `d->irq_enable` on the clear-register read nor inventing writable status storage is justified.

The implementation must retain the common tail: **zero read data, successful handler return `1`**. Removing `odata = d->irq_enable`, retaining `break`, and leaving emulated state unchanged—apart from the diagnostic latch—is the correct shape.

FAULT is **not available simply by copying `dev_unreadable`**. That handler returns `0`; [memory_rw.c:603](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:603) dispatches device-access exceptions for MIPS and M88K, with no ARM branch. ARM load/store callers assume an exception was already generated. ARM does have abort machinery in `memory_arm.c`, but this device-failure path does not invoke it. A return-code change would therefore not establish the proposed guest fault.

**Q1(b): There is no existing diagnostic-latch idiom in this file.** The structure at `dev_footbridge.c:60` contains no warning latch. `irq_asserted` tracks interrupt state; it is not an announcement latch.

I prefer a new `uint32_t warned_access` mask with named constants:

| Mask | Sole associated access |
|---|---|
| `0x01` | IRQ_STATUS write |
| `0x02` | IRQ_RAW_STATUS write |
| `0x04` | IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR read |
| `0x08` | FIQ_STATUS write |
| `0x10` | FIQ_RAW_STATUS write |

There are no existing writers of that field to collide with. DEVINIT’s `memset` initializes it. Use independent bits and `|=`; never clear it on an ordinary register access. Five `int` fields would also work, but “the file’s existing style” does not distinguish these alternatives.

**Q1(c): K1/K2 can establish the intended harness properties, but the current helpers need explicit integration.**

I checked all three implementations:

- [Sites `send()`](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/footbridge_sites_probe.py:225) ends with  
  `return wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None)`
- [Drain `send()`](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py:338) ends with  
  `return self.wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None, timeout=timeout)`
- [Witness `send()`](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_witness.py:268) has the same forwarding form as drain.

Their readers set `dead` on EOF or a caught `OSError`; their waits return failure on that condition before requiring an echo. Thus death is observable through an ordinary interaction while the descriptor remains open. A timeout alone returns failure without establishing `dead`.

Concrete requirements:

- **K1:** Parameterize each existing launcher’s hard-coded `-E cats`. Require no prompt, the diagnostic naming the supplied invalid name, and a normal exit with status exactly `1`. The actual diagnostic is `Sorry, emulation "<name>" is unknown.` (`machine.c:240`); `main.c:544` supplies `exit(1)`. Sites’ existing `fatals` collection only captures bracketed footbridge messages, so it cannot carry this evidence unchanged.
- **K2:** Require completed, checked prologue execution and a successful fresh `reg` response **from K2’s own session**. Require present register values: MMU bit clear, RAM `0x11223344`, VENDOR `0x1011`, and saved timer-mask result `0x00345678`. That last result is **r1 in sites, r7 in drain and witness**.
- Send `quit`, actually attempt the subsequent ordinary interaction, and snapshot its failure and `dead` **before cleanup**. Reap and require normal exit `0` afterward. Do not short-circuit away the interaction because `dead` is already set.
- Sites must capture these facts inside `run_arm()`: its current return happens after teardown and does not export a pre-cleanup `dead` observation.
- Sites currently drops `send()`’s timeout argument. Drain and witness forward it. A nominal short K2 interaction timeout therefore does not currently mean the same thing across the launchers.

Also, the shipped old K controls do **not** independently verify complete K-prologue execution: drain discards `step_and_check()`’s result, and witness’s K predicate does not require its recorded `badis`/`nsteps`. The new prerequisite must be scored explicitly.

Pin these separately: **sites-K1, sites-K2, drain-K1, drain-K2, witness-K1, witness-K2**. Together they establish startup-death detection and death detection after demonstrated execution. Device reachability remains the value rows’ responsibility.

**Q1(d): The live-exit count is nine, not ten.** These are all executable `exit(1)` calls in `DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)`:

| HEAD line | Reaching guest access | After this round |
|---|---|---|
| 565 | WRITE `+0x180`, IRQ_STATUS | Removed |
| 574 | WRITE `+0x184`, IRQ_RAW_STATUS | Removed |
| 606 | READ `+0x18c`, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR | Removed |
| 615 | WRITE `+0x280`, FIQ_STATUS | Removed |
| 624 | WRITE `+0x284`, FIQ_RAW_STATUS | Removed |
| 714 | READ `+0x100`, SDRAM_BA_MASK | Remains |
| 739 | READ `+0x104`, SDRAM_BA_OFFSET | Remains |

The other executable calls are **843** and **879**, both unsupported-machine paths in DEVINIT. References at **590** and **665** inside the access function are comments.

Consequently, the proposed function retains the SDRAM read pair; the file retains that pair plus the DEVINIT pair. For the stated probe programs, which exclude the SDRAM rows, “no footbridge access these probes make ends the process” is true **conditional on implementing the change and retiring every old K arm**. It is not a claim about every possible footbridge access.

**Q1(e): The permitted search found these complaint consumers:**

- Sites, line 402: `"ENABLE CLEAR"` in `K["fatals"]`.
- Drain, line 612: `"ENABLE CLEAR" in s.buf`.
- Witness, line 489: `"ENABLE CLEAR" in s.buf`.

I found no additional consumers of the other complaint texts in the scoped source/Python search.

**Gate compatibility remains unsettled.** Your read scope excludes `gate_arm.sh`, `gate_hygiene.sh`, and non-Python allowlists. Their relevant contents were not inlined. Sites’ comments at 208–212 explicitly describe a literal helper-call dependency in `gate_hygiene.sh`; that is evidence that such coupling needs checking, not verification of the current gate or its boot-log rules.

**Q2: These are predicted oracle outcomes, not measurements.** The new script is not present, so execution order cannot yet establish a literal “first” failing row.

| Weakening | Failing row or escape |
|---|---|
| **(a) One terminating call retained** | The corresponding D row fails continuation/survival and exit `0`. Each site needs its own pre-fix witness execution. |
| **(b) Unlatched announcement** | Corresponding D count becomes `2` instead of `1`. |
| **(c) Shared bit between sites** | **Escapes if each site runs in a fresh process.** In one device instance, the second site’s expected count becomes `0`. Require a mixed-site arm. |
| **(d) Retained enable-mask readback** | D3 receives `0x30` instead of `0`; its value check fails. |
| **(e) Declined write stores into `irq_status`** | D1/D2 state checks catch `0x10` if they inspect raw/status afterward. A FIQ-site write corrupting IRQ state escapes checks confined to FIQ; inspect both state families. |
| **(f) Legal IRQ_STATUS read becomes constant zero** | **Escapes A1 as proposed.** Its expected value is already zero. The analogous zero-state raw/FIQ rows have the same weakness. |
| **(g) Wrong register named** | Exact, access-local text matching fails the corresponding D row. Generic diagnostic counting fails to protect this; even complete-message counts over a combined transcript can miss a permutation of register names. |
| **(h) K1 accepts any nonzero exit** | K1 remains green on the ordinary correct run. Require a negative oracle check rejecting a matching-diagnostic child with another exit status, and rejecting signal death. |
| **(i) K2 accepts death before prologue completion** | Correct K2 fails its own execution/fresh-register prerequisites. A weakened predicate omitting them can remain green; another arm’s successful prologue provides no protection. |
| **(j) Read clears a latch** | Adjacent repeated writes can miss this. Interleave a legal read between repeated declines. D3 catches a reset on each declined read, but not every possible reset on another register’s read. |
| **(k) Legs implemented in one launcher only** | If the others retain old K, their K rows fail after the device fix. If old K is simply deleted there, omission escapes unless the gate requires every separately named launcher/leg result. |

The zero-state limitation also weakens “unchanged” claims: clearing an already-zero status or deasserting an already-deasserted output passes. Preservation needs a nonzero starting state and an observable interrupt-output check.

**Q3: The option set needs explicit treatment of the following distinctions.**

| Member | Disposition |
|---|---|
| DELETE | Removing a whole case loses its legal direction and exposes the unlatched default diagnostic. Removing only the bad-direction body is silent decline. |
| DECLINE | Viable. Sibling `FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR` reads and `TIMER_1_CLEAR` reads already follow the zero/no-side-effect shape. |
| DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE | Reasonable policy choice, subject to independent latches and adequate tests. |
| ACCEPT | The four RO writes deserve consideration as accepted no-effect accesses under the access legend. This never licenses storing the value. Existing computation of `irq_enable` does not justify accepting a read at `+0x18c`. |
| FAULT | Neither established by the cited hardware semantics nor supplied by the current ARM device-failure dispatcher. |
| BACKED ACCEPT | Invents status storage or clear-register readback semantics without a warrant. Reject. |
| IMPLEMENT | No missing documented operation has been identified for these directions. Broader interrupt-controller implementation is separate work. |

Two source findings prevent treating the proposed FIQ rows as straightforward IRQ copies:

- At [dev_footbridge.c:628](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_footbridge.c:628), reading `FIQ_ENABLE_SET` returns the initialized zero output; it does **not** expose `fiq_enable`. Writing `0xc0` does not give the proposed guest rows an enable-preservation oracle.
- `fiq_status` has no producer in this file after initialization. The interrupt callbacks update `irq_status` only, despite the datasheet’s identical-raw-status statement.

A focused handler fixture with seeded state can test FIQ preservation and nonzero legal reads without expanding this round into implementing FIQ. The guest rows still supply reachability and survival evidence. Name that split explicitly.

**Q4: The smallest credible vacuous change is moving cleanup before the death observation.**

Move `end = s.close()` ahead of the ordinary-interaction/dead check. **K2 can remain green**: `close()` itself sends `quit`, drains, closes the descriptor, and returns exit `0`; a later `send("reg")` catches the closed-descriptor error and marks `dead`.

That proves nothing about ordinary interaction detecting child death before cleanup. A useful negative control omits K2’s dedicated `quit`: the next `reg` must succeed, and K2 must fail even though eventual cleanup exits normally.

I found further harness corrections worth including:

- In [sites’ scoring](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/footbridge_sites_probe.py:353), T’s exit status is recorded but never required to equal `0`. Also, C4 accepts a missing vendor value because `None != 0x1011`.
- Existing row names already include **A1** for VENDOR, **A2** for the timer mask, and **D1** for disassembly. The proposed new meanings need distinct tags; K1’s old meaning must be explicitly retired.
- “False only on EOF/EIO” overstates the helpers: they catch **any `OSError`**. That makes preserving the pre-cleanup observation especially important.

The missing measurements I would require are independent baseline/post-fix D witnesses; one same-instance, interleaved latch sequence with access-local message checks; nonzero state/output preservation checks; and harness negative controls for wrong exit status, incomplete execution, and omitted K2 quit. None was run here.

I prefer a new `uint32_t warned_access` mask with named constants:

| Mask | Sole associated access |
|---|---|
| `0x01` | IRQ_STATUS write |
| `0x02` | IRQ_RAW_STATUS write |
| `0x04` | IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR read |
| `0x08` | FIQ_STATUS write |
| `0x10` | FIQ_RAW_STATUS write |

There are no existing writers of that field to collide with. DEVINIT’s `memset` initializes it. Use independent bits and `|=`; never clear it on an ordinary register access. Five `int` fields would also work, but “the file’s existing style” does not distinguish these alternatives.

**Q1(c): K1/K2 can establish the intended harness properties, but the current helpers need explicit integration.**

I checked all three implementations:

- [Sites `send()`](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/footbridge_sites_probe.py:225) ends with  
  `return wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None)`
- [Drain `send()`](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py:338) ends with  
  `return self.wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None, timeout=timeout)`
- [Witness `send()`](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_witness.py:268) has the same forwarding form as drain.

Their readers set `dead` on EOF or a caught `OSError`; their waits return failure on that condition before requiring an echo. Thus death is observable through an ordinary interaction while the descriptor remains open. A timeout alone returns failure without establishing `dead`.

Concrete requirements:

- **K1:** Parameterize each existing launcher’s hard-coded `-E cats`. Require no prompt, the diagnostic naming the supplied invalid name, and a normal exit with status exactly `1`. The actual diagnostic is `Sorry, emulation "<name>" is unknown.` (`machine.c:240`); `main.c:544` supplies `exit(1)`. Sites’ existing `fatals` collection only captures bracketed footbridge messages, so it cannot carry this evidence unchanged.
- **K2:** Require completed, checked prologue execution and a successful fresh `reg` response **from K2’s own session**. Require present register values: MMU bit clear, RAM `0x11223344`, VENDOR `0x1011`, and saved timer-mask result `0x00345678`. That last result is **r1 in sites, r7 in drain and witness**.
- Send `quit`, actually attempt the subsequent ordinary interaction, and snapshot its failure and `dead` **before cleanup**. Reap and require normal exit `0` afterward. Do not short-circuit away the interaction because `dead` is already set.
- Sites must capture these facts inside `run_arm()`: its current return happens after teardown and does not export a pre-cleanup `dead` observation.
- Sites currently drops `send()`’s timeout argument. Drain and witness forward it. A nominal short K2 interaction timeout therefore does not currently mean the same thing across the launchers.

Also, the shipped old K controls do **not** independently verify complete K-prologue execution: drain discards `step_and_check()`’s result, and witness’s K predicate does not require its recorded `badis`/`nsteps`. The new prerequisite must be scored explicitly.

Pin these separately: **sites-K1, sites-K2, drain-K1, drain-K2, witness-K1, witness-K2**. Together they establish startup-death detection and death detection after demonstrated execution. Device reachability remains the value rows’ responsibility.

**Q1(d): The live-exit count is nine, not ten.** These are all executable `exit(1)` calls in `DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)`:

| HEAD line | Reaching guest access | After this round |
|---|---|---|
| 565 | WRITE `+0x180`, IRQ_STATUS | Removed |
| 574 | WRITE `+0x184`, IRQ_RAW_STATUS | Removed |
| 606 | READ `+0x18c`, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR | Removed |
| 615 | WRITE `+0x280`, FIQ_STATUS | Removed |
| 624 | WRITE `+0x284`, FIQ_RAW_STATUS | Removed |
| 714 | READ `+0x100`, SDRAM_BA_MASK | Remains |
| 739 | READ `+0x104`, SDRAM_BA_OFFSET | Remains |

The other executable calls are **843** and **879**, both unsupported-machine paths in DEVINIT. References at **590** and **665** inside the access function are comments.

Consequently, the proposed function retains the SDRAM read pair; the file retains that pair plus the DEVINIT pair. For the stated probe programs, which exclude the SDRAM rows, “no footbridge access these probes make ends the process” is true **conditional on implementing the change and retiring every old K arm**. It is not a claim about every possible footbridge access.

- Drain, line 612: `"ENABLE CLEAR" in s.buf`.
- Witness, line 489: `"ENABLE CLEAR" in s.buf`.

I found no additional consumers of the other complaint texts in the scoped source/Python search.

**Gate compatibility remains unsettled.** Your read scope excludes `gate_arm.sh`, `gate_hygiene.sh`, and non-Python allowlists. Their relevant contents were not inlined. Sites’ comments at 208–212 explicitly describe a literal helper-call dependency in `gate_hygiene.sh`; that is evidence that such coupling needs checking, not verification of the current gate or its boot-log rules.

**Q2: These are predicted oracle outcomes, not measurements.** The new script is not present, so execution order cannot yet establish a literal “first” failing row.

| Weakening | Failing row or escape |
|---|---|
| **(a) One terminating call retained** | The corresponding D row fails continuation/survival and exit `0`. Each site needs its own pre-fix witness execution. |
| **(b) Unlatched announcement** | Corresponding D count becomes `2` instead of `1`. |
| **(c) Shared bit between sites** | **Escapes if each site runs in a fresh process.** In one device instance, the second site’s expected count becomes `0`. Require a mixed-site arm. |
| **(d) Retained enable-mask readback** | D3 receives `0x30` instead of `0`; its value check fails. |
| **(e) Declined write stores into `irq_status`** | D1/D2 state checks catch `0x10` if they inspect raw/status afterward. A FIQ-site write corrupting IRQ state escapes checks confined to FIQ; inspect both state families. |
| **(f) Legal IRQ_STATUS read becomes constant zero** | **Escapes A1 as proposed.** Its expected value is already zero. The analogous zero-state raw/FIQ rows have the same weakness. |
| **(g) Wrong register named** | Exact, access-local text matching fails the corresponding D row. Generic diagnostic counting fails to protect this; even complete-message counts over a combined transcript can miss a permutation of register names. |
| **(h) K1 accepts any nonzero exit** | K1 remains green on the ordinary correct run. Require a negative oracle check rejecting a matching-diagnostic child with another exit status, and rejecting signal death. |
| **(i) K2 accepts death before prologue completion** | Correct K2 fails its own execution/fresh-register prerequisites. A weakened predicate omitting them can remain green; another arm’s successful prologue provides no protection. |
| **(j) Read clears a latch** | Adjacent repeated writes can miss this. Interleave a legal read between repeated declines. D3 catches a reset on each declined read, but not every possible reset on another register’s read. |
| **(k) Legs implemented in one launcher only** | If the others retain old K, their K rows fail after the device fix. If old K is simply deleted there, omission escapes unless the gate requires every separately named launcher/leg result. |

The zero-state limitation also weakens “unchanged” claims: clearing an already-zero status or deasserting an already-deasserted output passes. Preservation needs a nonzero starting state and an observable interrupt-output check.

**Q3: The option set needs explicit treatment of the following distinctions.**

| Member | Disposition |
|---|---|
| DELETE | Removing a whole case loses its legal direction and exposes the unlatched default diagnostic. Removing only the bad-direction body is silent decline. |
| DECLINE | Viable. Sibling `FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR` reads and `TIMER_1_CLEAR` reads already follow the zero/no-side-effect shape. |
| DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE | Reasonable policy choice, subject to independent latches and adequate tests. |
| ACCEPT | The four RO writes deserve consideration as accepted no-effect accesses under the access legend. This never licenses storing the value. Existing computation of `irq_enable` does not justify accepting a read at `+0x18c`. |
| FAULT | Neither established by the cited hardware semantics nor supplied by the current ARM device-failure dispatcher. |
| BACKED ACCEPT | Invents status storage or clear-register readback semantics without a warrant. Reject. |
| IMPLEMENT | No missing documented operation has been identified for these directions. Broader interrupt-controller implementation is separate work. |

Two source findings prevent treating the proposed FIQ rows as straightforward IRQ copies:

- At [dev_footbridge.c:628](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_footbridge.c:628), reading `FIQ_ENABLE_SET` returns the initialized zero output; it does **not** expose `fiq_enable`. Writing `0xc0` does not give the proposed guest rows an enable-preservation oracle.
- `fiq_status` has no producer in this file after initialization. The interrupt callbacks update `irq_status` only, despite the datasheet’s identical-raw-status statement.

A focused handler fixture with seeded state can test FIQ preservation and nonzero legal reads without expanding this round into implementing FIQ. The guest rows still supply reachability and survival evidence. Name that split explicitly.

**Q4: The smallest credible vacuous change is moving cleanup before the death observation.**

Move `end = s.close()` ahead of the ordinary-interaction/dead check. **K2 can remain green**: `close()` itself sends `quit`, drains, closes the descriptor, and returns exit `0`; a later `send("reg")` catches the closed-descriptor error and marks `dead`.

That proves nothing about ordinary interaction detecting child death before cleanup. A useful negative control omits K2’s dedicated `quit`: the next `reg` must succeed, and K2 must fail even though eventual cleanup exits normally.

I found further harness corrections worth including:

- In [sites’ scoring](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/footbridge_sites_probe.py:353), T’s exit status is recorded but never required to equal `0`. Also, C4 accepts a missing vendor value because `None != 0x1011`.
- Existing row names already include **A1** for VENDOR, **A2** for the timer mask, and **D1** for disassembly. The proposed new meanings need distinct tags; K1’s old meaning must be explicitly retired.
- “False only on EOF/EIO” overstates the helpers: they catch **any `OSError`**. That makes preserving the pre-cleanup observation especially important.

The missing measurements I would require are independent baseline/post-fix D witnesses; one same-instance, interleaved latch sequence with access-local message checks; nonzero state/output preservation checks; and harness negative controls for wrong exit status, incomplete execution, and omitted K2 quit. None was run here.
